    {
      "id": "Konseijin adalah dewa Jepang yang dipuja melalui benda suci berbentuk falus. Ia berbeda dari Dōsojin, tetapi pemujaannya berpadu di beberapa daerah.",
      "en": "Konseijin is a Japanese deity worshipped through a phallic sacred object. He is distinct from Dōsojin but the worships merge in some regions.",

        "konseijin-c01",
        "konseijin-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaannya menghubungkan kesuburan dengan kelahiran, hubungan, dan kemakmuran. Kuilnya terutama banyak terdapat di Jepang timur.",

      ]
    }

      "title": "金精神",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "author": null

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "konseijin-c01",
      "source_id": "konseijin-s1",
      "quote": "金精神（こんせいしん）は、金精大明神（こんせいだいみょうじん）、金精様（こんせいさま）などとも呼ばれ、男根の形をした御神体を祀った神の一柱である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konseijin adalah dewa Jepang yang dipuja melalui benda suci berbentuk falus.",
        "en": "Konseijin is a Japanese deity worshipped through a phallic sacred object."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "konseijin-c02",
      "source_id": "konseijin-s1",
      "quote": "同じく男根の形をした御神体を祀った道祖神（塞の神）と混同されることが多いが、基本的に男根形の道祖神（塞の神）と金精神は異なる神である。しかし、栃木県などの一部の地域では金精神と道祖神が習合してしまっている例が多い。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berbeda dari Dōsojin, tetapi pemujaannya berpadu di beberapa daerah.",
        "en": "He is distinct from Dōsojin but the worships merge in some regions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "konseijin-c03",
      "source_id": "konseijin-s1",
      "quote": "金精神は、豊穣や生産に結びつく性器崇拝の信仰によるものから始まったとされている。子宝、安産、縁結び、下の病や性病などに霊験があるとされるが、他に豊穣や生産に結びつくことから商売繁盛にも霊験があるとされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya menghubungkan kesuburan dengan kelahiran, hubungan, dan kemakmuran.",
        "en": "The worship links fertility with childbirth, relationships and prosperity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "konseijin-c04",
      "source_id": "konseijin-s1",
      "quote": "金精神を祀った神社は全国各地にあるが、特に東日本の東北地方から関東地方にかけての地域に多くみられる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuilnya terutama banyak terdapat di Jepang timur.",
        "en": "His shrines are especially common in eastern Japan."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kotobuki adalah yōkai dalam mitologi Jepang. Tubuh gabungannya memadukan ciri dua belas hewan zodiak. Kepalanya memadukan kepala tikus dengan telinga kelinci dan tanduk sapi.",
      "en": "Kotobuki is a yōkai in Japanese mythology. Its composite body combines features of all twelve zodiac animals. Its head combines a rat’s head with rabbit ears and ox horns.",

        "kotobuki-folklore-c01",
        "kotobuki-folklore-c02",

      "source_id": "kotobuki-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The Kotobuki is a Japanese Chimera that has the parts of the creatures of the animals on the Chinese zodiac where it sports the head of a rat, the ears of a rabbit, the horns of an ox, the comb of a rooster, the beard of a goat, the neck of a dragon, the mane of a horse, the shoulders of a tiger, the arms of a monkey, the back of a boar, the hind legs of a dog, and the tail of a snake.",

    {
      "id": "kotobuki-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "kotobuki-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "the back of a boar, the hind legs of a dog, and the tail of a snake.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memiliki punggung babi hutan, kaki belakang anjing, dan ekor ular.",
        "en": "It has a boar’s back, dog hind legs and a snake tail."

    {
      "id": "kotobuki-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "kotobuki-folklore-s2",

    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kumano Gongen menunjuk dewa tiga kuil utama Kumano dalam kerangka sinkretis. Kelompok itu dapat menunjuk tiga dewa utama atau himpunan lebih luas dua belas dewa.",
      "en": "Kumano Gongen denotes deities of Kumano’s three major shrines within a syncretic framework. The group can refer to three chief deities or the broader twelve-deity set.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kumano-gongen-c01",
        "kumano-gongen-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tiga pusat kuil berpadu dengan saling memuja dewa mereka. Padanan Buddha para dewa itu mencakup Amitabha, Yakushi, dan Kannon berlengan seribu.",
      "en": "The three shrine centers integrate by mutually enshrining their deities. The deities’ Buddhist counterparts include Amitabha, Yakushi and thousand-armed Kannon.",

        "kumano-gongen-c04"
      ]

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%86%8A%E9%87%8E%E6%A8%A9%E7%8F%BE",
      "title": "熊野権現",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kumano-gongen-c01",
      "source_id": "kumano-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "熊野権現（くまのごんげん、または熊野神〈くまののかみ〉、熊野大神〈くまののおおかみ〉とも）は、熊野三山に祀られる神であり、本地垂迹思想のもとで権現と呼ばれるようになった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kumano Gongen menunjuk dewa tiga kuil utama Kumano dalam kerangka sinkretis.",
        "en": "Kumano Gongen denotes deities of Kumano’s three major shrines within a syncretic framework."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumano-gongen-c02",
      "source_id": "kumano-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "熊野権現とは熊野三山の祭神である神々をいい、特に主祭神である家津美御子（けつみみこ）（スサノオ）・速玉（イザナギ）・牟須美（ふすび、むすび、または「結」とも表記）（イザナミ）のみを指して熊野三所権現、熊野三所権現以外の神々も含めて熊野十二所権現ともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kelompok itu dapat menunjuk tiga dewa utama atau himpunan lebih luas dua belas dewa.",
        "en": "The group can refer to three chief deities or the broader twelve-deity set."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumano-gongen-c03",
      "source_id": "kumano-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "以上のように、12世紀末までに三山が互いの祭神を祀りあうことにより、三山は一体化を遂げたのである。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tiga pusat kuil berpadu dengan saling memuja dewa mereka.",
        "en": "The three shrine centers integrate by mutually enshrining their deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumano-gongen-c04",
      "source_id": "kumano-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "熊野本宮大社の主祭神の家都御子神（けつみこのかみ）または家都美御子神（けつみみこのかみ）は阿弥陀如来、新宮の熊野速玉大社の熊野速玉男神（くまのはやたまおのかみ）または速玉神（はやたまのかみ）は薬師如来、熊野那智大社の熊野牟須美神（くまのむすみのかみ）または夫須美神（ふすみのかみ）は千手観音とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan Buddha para dewa itu mencakup Amitabha, Yakushi, dan Kannon berlengan seribu.",
        "en": "The deities’ Buddhist counterparts include Amitabha, Yakushi and thousand-armed Kannon."
      }
    }
  ],

    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaan menghubungkan dewa itu dengan hujan, irigasi, dan sumur. Terjemahan universitas menyebut Kuraokami yang disusul Kuramitsuha. Terjemahan itu menghitung Kuramitsuha di antara delapan dewa yang lahir dari pedang.",
      "en": "Worship connects the deity with rain, irrigation and wells. The university translation names Kuraokami followed by Kuramitsuha. It counts Kuramitsuha among eight deities born from the sword.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kuramitsuha-c04",
        "kuramitsuha-c05",
        "kuramitsuha-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-s1",

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%82%AF%E3%83%A9%E3%83%9F%E3%83%84%E3%83%8F",
      "title": "クラミツハ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://kojiki.kokugakuin.ac.jp/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/08-Studies-on-the-Kojiki-Chapter-8-The-Slaying-of-the-Fire-Deity.pdf",
      "title": "08-Studies-on-the-Kojiki-Chapter-8-The-Slaying-of-the-Fire-Deity.pdf",
      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-c01",
      "source_id": "kuramitsuha-s1",
      "quote": "日本神話では、神産みにおいて、伊邪那岐命が火之迦具土神を斬り殺した際に生まれたとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuramitsuha adalah dewa Jepang yang lahir ketika Izanagi membunuh Kagutsuchi.",
        "en": "Kuramitsuha is a Japanese deity born when Izanagi slays Kagutsuchi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-c02",
      "source_id": "kuramitsuha-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』及び『日本書紀』の一書では、迦具土神を斬った御刀の柄に溜まった血が、指の間から漏れ出て、闇淤加美神（くらおかみのかみ）とともに闇御津羽神が生まれ、『日本書紀』の一書では軻遇突智命を斬って生じた三柱の神のうちの一柱が高龗神（たかおかみのかみ）であるとしている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menyebut darah gagang pedang menghasilkan Kuramitsuha bersama Kuraokami.",
        "en": "The Kojiki has blood from the sword handle produce Kuramitsuha with Kuraokami."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-c03",
      "source_id": "kuramitsuha-s1",
      "quote": "「闇」は「暗」で暗がりである谷間を、「ミツハ」は「水早」で水の出始めを意味する。これは弥都波能売神の罔象（ミツハ）と同意味。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya ditafsirkan melalui lembah gelap dan air yang muncul.",
        "en": "The name is interpreted through a dark valley and water emerging."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-c04",
      "source_id": "kuramitsuha-s1",
      "quote": "その為、闇罔象神は祈雨（きう）、止雨（しう）、灌漑の神、井戸の神として信仰されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan menghubungkan dewa itu dengan hujan, irigasi, dan sumur.",
        "en": "Worship connects the deity with rain, irrigation and wells."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-c05",
      "source_id": "kuramitsuha-s2",
      "quote": "The deity that thereupon came into existence was named Kura okami no kami 闇淤加美 神. Next [appeared] Kura mitsuha no kami 闇御津羽神 (8).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan universitas menyebut Kuraokami yang disusul Kuramitsuha.",
        "en": "The university translation names Kuraokami followed by Kuramitsuha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kuramitsuha-c06",
      "source_id": "kuramitsuha-s2",
      "quote": "The deities from Iwasaku no kami to Kura mitsuha no kami, eight deities altogether, were all born from the sword.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan itu menghitung Kuramitsuha di antara delapan dewa yang lahir dari pedang.",
        "en": "It counts Kuramitsuha among eight deities born from the sword."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kurayarō adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
    "en": "Kurayarō is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kurayaro-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kurayarō adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Ia berupa pelana bermata dan bertaring, memegang cambuk dengan tangan mirip tali. Satu tafsir menghubungkannya dengan pelana pengikut yang dikhianati, Kamata Masakiyo.",
      "en": "Kurayarō is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. It is a saddle with eyes and fangs, holding a whip with cord-like hands. One interpretation connects it with the betrayed retainer Kamata Masakiyo’s saddle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kurayaro-c01",
        "kurayaro-c02",
        "kurayaro-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Peneliti mengaitkan pasangannya dengan Abumiguchi pada bagian Tsurezuregusa tentang perlengkapan kuda. Kisah belakangan menyebut pelana menjadi hidup dan bertindak seperti prajurit. Kisah itu menyebut pelana Masakiyo mengayunkan tongkat dan bertarung.",
      "en": "Researchers relate its pairing with Abumiguchi to a Tsurezuregusa passage about horse gear. A later account says the saddles become alive and behave as warriors. That account has Masakiyo’s saddle brandish sticks and fight.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kurayaro-c04",
        "kurayaro-c05",
        "kurayaro-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],

    {

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kurayaro-c01",
      "source_id": "kurayaro-s1",
      "quote": "鞍野郎（くらやろう）は、鳥山石燕による江戸時代の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurayarō adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Kurayarō is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurayaro-c02",
      "source_id": "kurayaro-s1",
      "quote": "鞍（くら、馬具のひとつ）の妖怪であり、鞍に牙と目が生えた形で、紐状の手には鞭を持っている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berupa pelana bermata dan bertaring, memegang cambuk dengan tangan mirip tali.",
        "en": "It is a saddle with eyes and fangs, holding a whip with cord-like hands."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurayaro-c03",
      "source_id": "kurayaro-s1",
      "quote": "とあり、策略によって主君共々殺害された源氏の家臣・鎌田政清（かまた まさきよ）が使用していた馬の鞍が変化して生まれた妖怪であると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menghubungkannya dengan pelana pengikut yang dikhianati, Kamata Masakiyo.",
        "en": "One interpretation connects it with the betrayed retainer Kamata Masakiyo’s saddle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurayaro-c04",
      "source_id": "kurayaro-s1",
      "quote": "また、石燕は鐙口とおなじ見開きに鞍野郎を描いており、この2体は『徒然草』に鞍など馬具について注意しろとする内容（186段）が登場することをモチーフにして創作されているとも考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti mengaitkan pasangannya dengan Abumiguchi pada bagian Tsurezuregusa tentang perlengkapan kuda.",
        "en": "Researchers relate its pairing with Abumiguchi to a Tsurezuregusa passage about horse gear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurayaro-c05",
      "source_id": "kurayaro-s2",
      "quote": "They take on lives of their own and act like warriors.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan menyebut pelana menjadi hidup dan bertindak seperti prajurit.",
        "en": "A later account says the saddles become alive and behave as warriors."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kurayaro-c06",
      "source_id": "kurayaro-s2",
      "quote": "Afterwards, his saddle would pick up sticks and prance about like a warrior, fighting everything it could.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah itu menyebut pelana Masakiyo mengayunkan tongkat dan bertarung.",
        "en": "That account has Masakiyo’s saddle brandish sticks and fight."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Majimun is a collective name for evil spirits and yōkai in Okinawa and Amami.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "majimun-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Majimun adalah nama kolektif roh jahat dan yōkai di Okinawa serta Amami. Tradisi memperingatkan bahwa majimun berwujud hewan yang melewati sela kaki seseorang membawa kematian.",
      "en": "Majimun is a collective name for evil spirits and yōkai in Okinawa and Amami. Tradition warns that an animal-shaped majimun passing between a person’s legs brings death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "majimun-c01",
        "majimun-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi berbentuk bebek diusir tetapi berubah menjadi kunang-kunang sebelum menghilang pada suara ayam jantan. Versi bebek dapat ditafsirkan sebagai roh orang yang sakit berat.",
      "en": "A duck-shaped version is driven away but becomes fireflies before disappearing at a rooster’s call. The duck version can be interpreted as the living spirit of a seriously ill person.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "majimun-c03",
        "majimun-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],

  "modern_depictions": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "majimun-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%9E%E3%82%B8%E3%83%A0%E3%83%B3",
      "title": "マジムン",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "majimun-c01",
      "source_id": "majimun-s1",
      "quote": "マジムンは、沖縄県や鹿児島県奄美群島に伝わる悪霊、妖怪の総称。様々なマジムンが伝えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Majimun adalah nama kolektif roh jahat dan yōkai di Okinawa serta Amami.",
        "en": "Majimun is a collective name for evil spirits and yōkai in Okinawa and Amami."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "majimun-c02",
      "source_id": "majimun-s1",
      "quote": "動物の姿をしたマジムンに股をくぐられると死んでしまうことから、決して股をくぐられてはいけないといわれる。また奄美群島の一部ではハブのことをマジムンと呼び、伝承では神の使いであるともされ、マジムンの中では唯一実在する生物である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi memperingatkan bahwa majimun berwujud hewan yang melewati sela kaki seseorang membawa kematian.",
        "en": "Tradition warns that an animal-shaped majimun passing between a person’s legs brings death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "majimun-c03",
      "source_id": "majimun-s1",
      "quote": "動物のマジムンと同じく、股をくぐられると死んでしまうといわれる。 アフィラーマジムン アヒルの姿をしたマジムン。 沖縄県立読谷高等学校では、かつて運動場の前が谷底のようになっており、そこにアフィラーマジムンが現れたという。また、ある農民が夜中にアフィラーマジムンに出遭い、マジムンがしきりに股をくぐろうとするので、石を投げつけたところ、無数のホタルとなって農民の周りを飛び回り、ニワトリの声とともに消え去ったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi berbentuk bebek diusir tetapi berubah menjadi kunang-kunang sebelum menghilang pada suara ayam jantan.",
        "en": "A duck-shaped version is driven away but becomes fireflies before disappearing at a rooster’s call."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "majimun-c04",
      "source_id": "majimun-s1",
      "quote": "片脚のないアヒルの姿であり、重病人の生霊が化けたものともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi bebek dapat ditafsirkan sebagai roh orang yang sakit berat.",
        "en": "The duck version can be interpreted as the living spirit of a seriously ill person."
      }
    }
  ],

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%8C%AF",
      "title": "猯",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mami-c01",
      "source_id": "mami-s1",
      "quote": "また江戸時代の奇談集『絵本百物語』によれば、猯が老いて妖怪化したものが同書にある妖怪・野鉄砲とされる。同じく江戸時代の随筆『耳嚢』3巻では、江戸の番町に猯が現れたとあり、体色は鼠色、目は太陽か月のようで、杖で叩くとガマガエルの背のような感触だったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mami muncul dalam kisah gaib Jepang dan konon menjadi yōkai karena usia.",
        "en": "Mami appears in Japanese supernatural accounts and is said to become a yōkai with age."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mami-c02",
      "source_id": "mami-s1",
      "quote": "猯、魔魅（まみ）は、タヌキまたはニホンアナグマのこと。ただし、イノシシなど他の動物を指すこともあり、ムササビ、モモンガも「猯」と呼ばれたことがある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama itu juga menunjuk tanuki, luak, dan kadang hewan lain.",
        "en": "The name also denotes tanuki, badgers and sometimes other animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mami-c03",
      "source_id": "mami-s1",
      "quote": "一方で江戸時代の百科事典『和漢三才図会』では、「猯」は「狸」とは別種の動物として別々に掲載されている。同書では中国の本草学研究書『本草綱目』からの引用として、山中の穴に住んでいる肥えた獣で、褐色の短い毛に体を覆われ、耳が聞こえず、人の姿を見ると逃げようとするが行動は鈍いとある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ensiklopedia Edo membedakannya dari tanuki sebagai hewan gunung berbulu cokelat.",
        "en": "An Edo encyclopedia distinguishes it from tanuki as a brown-haired mountain animal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mami-c04",
      "source_id": "mami-s1",
      "quote": "同じく江戸時代の随筆『耳嚢』3巻では、江戸の番町に猯が現れたとあり、体色は鼠色、目は太陽か月のようで、杖で叩くとガマガエルの背のような感触だったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Edo menggambarkan makhluk kelabu dengan mata seperti matahari atau bulan.",
        "en": "An Edo account describes a gray creature with eyes like the sun or moon."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mikahayahi adalah dewa Jepang yang lahir dari darah pedang setelah kematian Kagutsuchi.",
    "en": "Mikahayahi is a Japanese deity born from sword blood after Kagutsuchi’s death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mikahayahi adalah dewa Jepang yang lahir dari darah pedang setelah kematian Kagutsuchi. Versi Nihon Shoki menjadikannya leluhur Takemikazuchi atau salah satu dari tiga dewa yang lahir bersama.",
      "en": "Mikahayahi is a Japanese deity born from sword blood after Kagutsuchi’s death. Nihon Shoki versions make him an ancestor of Takemikazuchi or one of three gods born together.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c01",
        "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Catatan wilayah Edo menyebutnya dewa bintang leluhur di Kuil Matsuzaki. Terjemahan universitas mengelompokkan Mikahayahi bersama Hihayahi dan Takemikazuchi. Komentar menafsirkan namanya melalui luhur, perkasa, tergesa, dan roh.",
      "en": "An Edo gazetteer describes him as an ancestral star deity at Matsuzaki Shrine. The university translation groups Mikahayahi with Hihayahi and Takemikazuchi. The commentary interprets his name through august, mighty, impetuous and spirit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c03",
        "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c04",
        "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

    {
      "id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-s2",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c01",
      "source_id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "甕速日神（みかはやひのかみ）とは、日本神話の神産みにおいてイザナギがカグツチの首を切り落とした際、十束剣「天之尾羽張（あめのおはばり）」の根元についた血が岩に飛び散って生まれた三神の一柱である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mikahayahi adalah dewa Jepang yang lahir dari darah pedang setelah kematian Kagutsuchi.",
        "en": "Mikahayahi is a Japanese deity born from sword blood after Kagutsuchi’s death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c02",
      "source_id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』には武甕雷男神の先祖であるとも記されているが、その後に樋速日神、甕速日神、武甕雷男神が同時に生まれたとも記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Nihon Shoki menjadikannya leluhur Takemikazuchi atau salah satu dari tiga dewa yang lahir bersama.",
        "en": "Nihon Shoki versions make him an ancestor of Takemikazuchi or one of three gods born together."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c03",
      "source_id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "江戸時代に松江藩で編纂された地誌『雲陽誌』の松崎神社の項において「当社の祖神を甕速日といふ星の神と名つく」と記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan wilayah Edo menyebutnya dewa bintang leluhur di Kuil Matsuzaki.",
        "en": "An Edo gazetteer describes him as an ancestral star deity at Matsuzaki Shrine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c04",
      "source_id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-s2",
      "quote": "The deity that thereupon came into existence was named Mikahayahi no kami 甕 速 日 神 . Next [appeared] Hihayahi no kami 樋 速 日 神 . Next Takemikazuchinoo no kami 建御雷之男神.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan universitas mengelompokkan Mikahayahi bersama Hihayahi dan Takemikazuchi.",
        "en": "The university translation groups Mikahayahi with Hihayahi and Takemikazuchi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-c05",
      "source_id": "mikahayahi-no-mikoto-s2",
      "quote": "The name Mikahayahi no kami 甕速日神 can be thought of as a composite of mi (“august”), ika (“mighty”), haya (“impetuous”), and hi (“spirit”).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komentar menafsirkan namanya melalui luhur, perkasa, tergesa, dan roh.",
        "en": "The commentary interprets his name through august, mighty, impetuous and spirit."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mino-bi adalah cahaya gaib di Shiga yang muncul pada jas hujan jerami ketika perjalanan perahu dalam hujan.",
    "en": "Mino-bi is a ghost light in Shiga that appears on straw raincoats during rainy boat journeys.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "mino-bi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mino-bi adalah cahaya gaib di Shiga yang muncul pada jas hujan jerami ketika perjalanan perahu dalam hujan. Melepaskan jas hujan menghilangkannya, sedangkan mengibaskan membuat cahayanya bertambah. Penjelasannya berbeda antara roh orang tenggelam dan gas alam.",
      "en": "Mino-bi is a ghost light in Shiga that appears on straw raincoats during rainy boat journeys. Removing the raincoat disperses it, while brushing it makes the lights multiply. Explanations differ between drowned spirits and natural gas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mino-bi-c01",
        "mino-bi-c02",
        "mino-bi-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah lain menyebut para pendamping mungkin tidak melihat cahaya itu. Kisah belakangan juga mengaitkan fenomena itu dengan rubah, cerpelai, atau tanuki. Tafsir lainnya menyamakannya dengan kunang-kunang atau ulat kantong.",
      "en": "Other accounts say companions may fail to see the lights. A later account also attributes the phenomenon to foxes, weasels or tanuki. Other interpretations identify it with fireflies or bagworms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mino-bi-c04",
        "mino-bi-c05",
        "mino-bi-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mino-bi-c01",
      "source_id": "mino-bi-s1",
      "quote": "蓑火（みのび）は、近江国（現・滋賀県）彦根に伝わる怪火。 == 概要 == 旧暦五月の梅雨の夜などに、琵琶湖を人の乗った舟が渡ると、その者が雨具として身に着けている蓑に点々と、まるでホタルの光のように火の玉が現れる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mino-bi adalah cahaya gaib di Shiga yang muncul pada jas hujan jerami ketika perjalanan perahu dalam hujan.",
        "en": "Mino-bi is a ghost light in Shiga that appears on straw raincoats during rainy boat journeys."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mino-bi-c02",
      "source_id": "mino-bi-s1",
      "quote": "蓑をすみやかに脱ぎ捨てれば蓑火も消えてしまうが、うかつに手で払いのけようとすれば、どんどん数を増し、星のまたたきのようにキラキラと光る。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melepaskan jas hujan menghilangkannya, sedangkan mengibaskan membuat cahayanya bertambah.",
        "en": "Removing the raincoat disperses it, while brushing it makes the lights multiply."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mino-bi-c03",
      "source_id": "mino-bi-s1",
      "quote": "琵琶湖で水死した人間の怨霊が姿を変えたものともいわれるが、井上円了の説によれば、これは一種のガスによる現象とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penjelasannya berbeda antara roh orang tenggelam dan gas alam.",
        "en": "Explanations differ between drowned spirits and natural gas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mino-bi-c04",
      "source_id": "mino-bi-s1",
      "quote": "信濃川流域に多いもので、主に雨の日の夜道や船上で蓑、傘、衣服に蛍状の火がまとわりつくもので、慌てて払うと火は勢いを増して体中を包み込むという。大勢でいるときでも一人にしか見えず、同行者には見えないことがあり、この状態は「蓑虫に憑かれた」と呼ばれる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menyebut para pendamping mungkin tidak melihat cahaya itu.",
        "en": "Other accounts say companions may fail to see the lights."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mino-bi-c05",
      "source_id": "mino-bi-s2",
      "quote": "Most often it is said to be the work of a mischievous kitsune, itachi, or tanuki.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah belakangan juga mengaitkan fenomena itu dengan rubah, cerpelai, atau tanuki.",
        "en": "A later account also attributes the phenomenon to foxes, weasels or tanuki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mino-bi-c06",
      "source_id": "mino-bi-s2",
      "quote": "sometimes minobi’s true form is believed to be a firefly or other insect, such as the minomushi (bagworm moth).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir lainnya menyamakannya dengan kunang-kunang atau ulat kantong.",
        "en": "Other interpretations identify it with fireflies or bagworms."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Belum ada berkas Wikimedia Commons yang diperiksa untuk entri ini."
    }
  ]
}

      "en": "Mino-waraji is a yōkai illustrated in Sekien’s collection. Its body is a straw raincoat, its legs are sandals and it carries a hoe.",

      "quote": "蓑草鞋（みのわらじ）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "source_id": "mino-waraji-s1",
      "quote": "雪のつもっている竹林の中を蓑（みの）が胴体、草鞋（わらじ）が両脚となり、鍬（くわ）をかついだ姿で描かれている。",

      "id": "mino-waraji-c03",
      "source_id": "mino-waraji-s1",
      "quote": "『百鬼夜行絵巻』や『付喪神絵巻』に草鞋や蓑をモチーフとして描かれた妖怪は存在しているので、それらから石燕が着想を得て描いたものであろうと考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",

    {
      "id": "mino-waraji-c04",
      "source_id": "mino-waraji-s1",
      "quote": "凶作が続いた時期に年貢を厳しく取り立てられた農民の怨みの念が、古い蓑や草鞋に乗り移って付喪神と化したものと解説されることもある。",

      "id": "Nai no Kami adalah dewa gempa dalam tradisi Jepang. Nihon Shoki mencatat pemujaannya setelah gempa pada 599.",
      "en": "Nai no Kami is an earthquake deity in Japanese tradition. The Nihon Shoki records worship after an earthquake in 599.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nai-no-kami-c01",
        "nai-no-kami-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama itu menunjuk kategori ilahi gempa alih-alih silsilah dewa tertentu. Pemujaan belakangan mengaitkan pencegahan gempa dengan Takemikazuchi, meskipun kronik tidak demikian.",
      "en": "The name denotes the earthquake divine category rather than a specific named genealogy. Later worship links earthquake prevention with Takemikazuchi, although the chronicles do not.",
      "claim_ids": [

      ]

      "source_id": "nai-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "ないの神（ないのかみ、なゐの神）は、日本神話に登場する地震の神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nai no Kami adalah dewa gempa dalam tradisi Jepang.",
        "en": "Nai no Kami is an earthquake deity in Japanese tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nai-no-kami-c02",
      "source_id": "nai-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "『日本書紀』の「推古天皇紀」に、推古天皇7年（599年）夏に大和地方を中心とする大地震（推古地震）があり、その後、諸国に「地震神（なゐのかみ）」を祀らせたとある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nihon Shoki mencatat pemujaannya setelah gempa pada 599.",
        "en": "The Nihon Shoki records worship after an earthquake in 599."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nai-no-kami-c03",
      "source_id": "nai-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "「なゐ」は地震のことであるので、これは神名ではなく「野の神」・「海の神」のような神格を表したものである。神名や出自などは記されていない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama itu menunjuk kategori ilahi gempa alih-alih silsilah dewa tertentu.",
        "en": "The name denotes the earthquake divine category rather than a specific named genealogy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nai-no-kami-c04",
      "source_id": "nai-no-kami-s1",
      "quote": "後に鹿島神宮にある要石（かなめいし）が地震をおさえているとの伝承から、鹿島神宮の祭神であるタケミカヅチが地震を防ぐ神とされるようになったが、『記紀』にはタケミカヅチと地震を関連づけるような記述はない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan belakangan mengaitkan pencegahan gempa dengan Takemikazuchi, meskipun kronik tidak demikian.",
        "en": "Later worship links earthquake prevention with Takemikazuchi, although the chronicles do not."
      }

      "claim_ids": [
        "noteppo-c01",
        "noteppo-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Teks buku menyebutnya menutupi wajah korban, sementara ilustrasinya melepaskan makhluk mirip kelelawar. Kisah menghubungkan asalnya dengan mami yang tua.",
      "en": "The book’s text covers a victim’s face, while its illustration sends out batlike creatures. The account associates its origin with an aged mami.",
      "claim_ids": [

      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "noteppo-c01",
      "source_id": "noteppo-s1",
      "quote": "野鉄砲（のでっぽう）は、江戸時代の奇談集『絵本百物語』にある妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Noteppō adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan Edo Ehon Hyakumonogatari.",
        "en": "Noteppō is a yōkai in the Edo collection Ehon Hyakumonogatari."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "noteppo-c02",
      "source_id": "noteppo-s1",
      "quote": "外見はタヌキ、リス、ムササビのようで、北国の山中の谷間や森の奥に棲んでいるという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyerupai beberapa mamalia kecil dan mendiami lembah gunung serta hutan utara.",
        "en": "It resembles several small mammals and inhabits northern mountain valleys and forests."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "noteppo-c03",
      "source_id": "noteppo-s1",
      "quote": "人を襲うときにはその人の視界を奪うというが、『絵本百物語』の本文によると自ら人の顔に覆いかぶさって目をふさぐとあり、同書の挿絵中の文章では、口からコウモリのようなものを放って人の顔にかぶせるとある（画像参照）。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks buku menyebutnya menutupi wajah korban, sementara ilustrasinya melepaskan makhluk mirip kelelawar.",
        "en": "The book’s text covers a victim’s face, while its illustration sends out batlike creatures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "noteppo-c04",
      "source_id": "noteppo-s1",
      "quote": "『絵本百物語』の本文によれば、猯（まみ）という獣が老いて妖怪化したものが野鉄砲であり、コウモリが老いて妖怪化した野衾と同一であるともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah menghubungkan asalnya dengan mami yang tua.",
        "en": "The account associates its origin with an aged mami."
      }
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nyubachi-bo-c01",
      "source_id": "nyubachi-bo-s1",
      "quote": "乳鉢坊（にゅうばちぼう）は、鳥山石燕 『百器徒然袋』に描かれている日本の妖怪で、銅盤の妖怪である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyūbachi-bō adalah yōkai alat perkusi logam yang digambar Sekien.",
        "en": "Nyūbachi-bō is a metal-percussion yōkai illustrated by Sekien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyubachi-bo-c02",
      "source_id": "nyubachi-bo-s1",
      "quote": "銅盤を頭にかぶった人のような姿で、瓢箪の妖怪である瓢箪小僧と共に描かれている。室町時代の『百鬼夜行絵巻』にも同じ形状な妖怪が描かれているので、これをモデルとして石燕が描いて名づけた妖怪であろうと考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memakai piring logam di kepala dan muncul bersama Hyōtan-kozō, mengacu pada gulungan lebih tua.",
        "en": "It wears a metal plate on its head and appears with Hyōtan-kozō, echoing an older scroll."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyubachi-bo-c03",
      "source_id": "nyubachi-bo-s1",
      "quote": "銅盤は芝居の鳴り物にも使われる楽器で、鐃鈸（にょうはち、にゅうばち）あるいは銅鈸子（どうばつし）・銅拍子とも呼ばれる。本来は寺社などで僧侶が法会をおこなう時などに使用していた金属製の輸入楽器である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alat itu terkait dengan pertunjukan dan upacara Buddha.",
        "en": "The instrument is associated with performances and Buddhist ceremonies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nyubachi-bo-c04",
      "source_id": "nyubachi-bo-s1",
      "quote": "どのような妖怪か他の鳥山石燕の描いている妖怪たち同様に明確ではない点が多いが、平成以降は、大きな音を鳴らして人を驚かせるなどと解説されていることが多い。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian modern menambahkan kemampuan mengejutkan orang dengan suara keras.",
        "en": "Modern descriptions add startling people with loud noise."

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ōgotooshio adalah dewa dalam mitologi Jepang. Kojiki menjadikannya dewa pertama yang lahir setelah Izanagi dan Izanami selesai menghasilkan negeri.",
      "en": "Ōgotooshio is a deity in Japanese mythology. The Kojiki makes him the first deity born after Izanagi and Izanami finish producing the land.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ogotooshio-c01",
        "ogotooshio-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Norinaga menafsirkan penempatannya sebagai kekeliruan yang melibatkan Kotosakanoo. Terjemahan universitas menempatkan kelahiran dewa setelah negeri selesai dibentuk. Terjemahan itu secara tegas mencantumkan Ōkoto Oshio pertama.",
      "en": "Norinaga interprets his placement as an error involving Kotosakanoo. The university translation sets the divine births after completion of the land. The translation explicitly lists Ōkoto Oshio first.",

        "ogotooshio-c04",
        "ogotooshio-c05"
      ]

  "cultural_context": null,

    {
      "id": "ogotooshio-s2",

    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ogotooshio-c01",
      "source_id": "ogotooshio-s1",
      "quote": "大事忍男神（おおごとおしおのかみ／おほごとおしをのかみ）は、日本神話に登場する神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ōgotooshio adalah dewa dalam mitologi Jepang.",
        "en": "Ōgotooshio is a deity in Japanese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogotooshio-c02",
      "source_id": "ogotooshio-s1",
      "quote": "大事忍男神（おおごとおしおのかみ／おほごとおしをのかみ）は、日本神話に登場する神である。 == 概要 == 古事記で、イザナギとイザナミが国産みを終えて神産みの最初に産んだ神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojiki menjadikannya dewa pertama yang lahir setelah Izanagi dan Izanami selesai menghasilkan negeri.",
        "en": "The Kojiki makes him the first deity born after Izanagi and Izanami finish producing the land."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogotooshio-c03",
      "source_id": "ogotooshio-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記伝』は、大事忍男神は熊野本宮大社に祭られる事解之男神のことであり、本来は黄泉から帰還したイザナギの禊祓に現れるべき神を誤って神産みの最初に入れてしまったのであろうと解釈している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Norinaga menafsirkan penempatannya sebagai kekeliruan yang melibatkan Kotosakanoo.",
        "en": "Norinaga interprets his placement as an error involving Kotosakanoo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogotooshio-c04",
      "source_id": "ogotooshio-s2",
      "quote": "After they had finished giving birth to the land, [Izanaki and Izanami] gave birth to deities.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan universitas menempatkan kelahiran dewa setelah negeri selesai dibentuk.",
        "en": "The university translation sets the divine births after completion of the land."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ogotooshio-c05",
      "source_id": "ogotooshio-s2",
      "quote": "The name of the [first] deity they bore is Ōkoto oshio no kami 大事忍男神 (1).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan itu secara tegas mencantumkan Ōkoto Oshio pertama.",
        "en": "The translation explicitly lists Ōkoto Oshio first."
      }
    }

      "id": "okaburo-c03",
      "source_id": "okaburo-s1",
      "quote": "一般的には八百歳とされることが多いが、能『菊慈童』では「七百歳」という語句が用いられており、石燕が大禿に付した文は、画題としても親しまれていた『菊慈童』を踏まえたパロディとみることができる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komentarnya ditafsirkan sebagai parodi bocah krisan abadi.",
        "en": "Its commentary is interpreted as a parody of the immortal chrysanthemum boy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okaburo-c04",
      "source_id": "okaburo-s1",
      "quote": "昭和後期以降、大きな顔の妖怪に「大かむろ」という名称が用いられており、石燕の「大禿」との混用が起こっているが、両者に直接関係は無く別々の妖怪である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran Ōkamuro belakangan adalah sosok berbeda meskipun namanya serupa.",
        "en": "Later Ōkamuro depictions are separate figures despite similar names."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Ōkamuzumi adalah dewa persik dalam mitologi Jepang. Izanagi memakai tiga persik untuk mengusir pasukan Yomi yang mengejarnya. Izanagi menamai persik itu dan memerintahkannya membantu orang yang kesusahan.",
      "en": "Ōkamuzumi is a peach deity in Japanese mythology. Izanagi uses three peaches to repel the pursuing Yomi forces. Izanagi names the peaches and commands them to aid distressed people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c01",
        "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c02",
        "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi Nihon Shoki menghubungkan persik dengan penolakan iblis. Ensiklopedia universitas juga mengidentifikasi persik bernama itu sebagai penolong Izanagi. Ensiklopedia menegaskan tiga persik yang membubarkan para pengejar.",
      "en": "A Nihon Shoki version connects the peaches with warding off demons. The university encyclopedia likewise identifies the named peaches as Izanagi’s rescuers. The encyclopedia confirms three peaches dispersing the pursuers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c04",
        "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c05",

  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

      "url": "https://d-museum.kokugakuin.ac.jp/eos/detail/?id=9351",

      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c01",
      "source_id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "オオカムヅミは、日本神話に登場する桃であり神。『古事記』では意富加牟豆美命（おおかむづみのみこと）と表記する。「大いなる神のミ（霊威）」の意味であるが、大いなる神の実と解釈し、「大神実命」と表記する場合もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ōkamuzumi adalah dewa persik dalam mitologi Jepang.",
        "en": "Ōkamuzumi is a peach deity in Japanese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c02",
      "source_id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "伊邪那岐命が、亡き妻の伊邪那美命を連れ戻そうと、死者の国である黄泉の国に赴くが、失敗して予母都色許売（よもつしこめ）や8柱の雷神、黄泉軍（よもついくさ）に追われる。地上との境にある黄泉比良坂（よもつひらさか）の麓まで逃げてきた時に、そこに生えていた桃の実を3個取って投げつけると、その霊力で雷神と黄泉軍は退散していった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Izanagi memakai tiga persik untuk mengusir pasukan Yomi yang mengejarnya.",
        "en": "Izanagi uses three peaches to repel the pursuing Yomi forces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c03",
      "source_id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "この功績により桃の実は、伊邪那岐命から「意富加牟豆美命（おおかむづみのみこと)」の神名を授けられ、「汝、我を助けしが如く、葦原中津国に有らゆるうつくしき青人草の、苦しき瀬に落ちて、患（うれ）へ悩むとき、これを助くべし（お前が私を助けてくれたように、葦原の中津国（地上世界）のあらゆる生ある人々が、苦しみの激流に落ち、悩み悲しみ苦しむことがあったときには、これを助けてやってくれ。）」と命じられた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Izanagi menamai persik itu dan memerintahkannya membantu orang yang kesusahan.",
        "en": "Izanagi names the peaches and commands them to aid distressed people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c04",
      "source_id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』と同様に、イザナギ命は黄泉の国で8柱の雷公に追われる。その時、道端に桃の樹があり、その樹の下に隠れて桃の実を採って投げつけると、雷公は退走していった。これが、桃を用いて鬼を避ける由縁であると記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Nihon Shoki menghubungkan persik dengan penolakan iblis.",
        "en": "A Nihon Shoki version connects the peaches with warding off demons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c05",
      "source_id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-s2",
      "quote": "The name given to the peaches that saved Izanagi during his flight from the underworld.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ensiklopedia universitas juga mengidentifikasi persik bernama itu sebagai penolong Izanagi.",
        "en": "The university encyclopedia likewise identifies the named peaches as Izanagi’s rescuers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-c06",
      "source_id": "okamuzumi-no-mikoto-s2",
      "quote": "when the underworld forces approached, he threw at them three peaches that were growing nearby, thus dispersing his pursuers.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ensiklopedia menegaskan tiga persik yang membubarkan para pengejar.",
        "en": "The encyclopedia confirms three peaches dispersing the pursuers."
      }
    }
  ],

  "countries": null,

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ōmiyanome adalah dewi Shinto yang menjaga kedamaian istana.",
    "en": "Ōmiyanome is a Shinto goddess protecting palace peace.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "omiyanome-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ōmiyanome adalah dewi Shinto yang menjaga kedamaian istana. Kogo Shūi menjadikannya anak Futodama. Ia melayani Amaterasu dan mendorong keselarasan melalui tutur kata indah.",
      "en": "Ōmiyanome is a Shinto goddess protecting palace peace. The Kogo Shūi makes her Futodama’s child. She serves Amaterasu and promotes harmony through gracious speech.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "omiyanome-c01",
        "omiyanome-c02",
        "omiyanome-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaannya mencakup usaha pelayanan tamu dan kerukunan rumah. Ōtono hogai mempersembahkan pemujaan kepada Ōmiyanome dan dua dewa lain demi perlindungan istana. Liturgi Engishiki ritual itu memberkati istana penobatan baru dengan kedamaian.",
      "en": "Her worship extends to hospitality businesses and household harmony. Ōtono hogai directs worship to Ōmiyanome and two other deities for palace protection. Its Engishiki liturgy blesses the new accession palace with peace.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "omiyanome-c04",
        "omiyanome-c05",
        "omiyanome-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%82%AA%E3%82%AA%E3%83%9F%E3%83%A4%E3%83%8E%E3%83%A1",
      "title": "オオミヤノメ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",

      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://d-museum.kokugakuin.ac.jp/eos/detail/?id=9120",
      "title": "Ōtono hogai | 國學院大學デジタルミュージアム",
      "publisher": "Kokugakuin University",
      "author": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-c01",
      "source_id": "omiyanome-s1",
      "quote": "オオミヤノメは、神道の神。『延喜式神名帳』と『古語拾遺』では大宮売神（おおみやのめのかみ）、伏見稲荷大社では大宮能売大神（おおみやのめのおおかみ）と表記する。宮殿の平安を守る女神である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ōmiyanome adalah dewi Shinto yang menjaga kedamaian istana.",
        "en": "Ōmiyanome is a Shinto goddess protecting palace peace."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-c02",
      "source_id": "omiyanome-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』や『日本書紀』には記されないが、『古語拾遺』に太玉命の子として登場する。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kogo Shūi menjadikannya anak Futodama.",
        "en": "The Kogo Shūi makes her Futodama’s child."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-c03",
      "source_id": "omiyanome-s1",
      "quote": "天岩戸から新殿に移った天照大神に侍女として仕えたとされ、内侍（女官）が善言美詞（麗しい言葉）を駆使して君臣の間を和らげるような働きをしたと記されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melayani Amaterasu dan mendorong keselarasan melalui tutur kata indah.",
        "en": "She serves Amaterasu and promotes harmony through gracious speech."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-c04",
      "source_id": "omiyanome-s1",
      "quote": "こうした性格から、旅館や百貨店など接客業の守護神として信仰されるほか、家内安全・家族和合の神ともされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya mencakup usaha pelayanan tamu dan kerukunan rumah.",
        "en": "Her worship extends to hospitality businesses and household harmony."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-c05",
      "source_id": "omiyanome-s2",
      "quote": "A court ritual directed at the three kami Yafunekukunochi no Mikoto , Yafune toyouke hime no Mikoto , and Ōmiyanome no Mikoto intended to protect the palace from disasters.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ōtono hogai mempersembahkan pemujaan kepada Ōmiyanome dan dua dewa lain demi perlindungan istana.",
        "en": "Ōtono hogai directs worship to Ōmiyanome and two other deities for palace protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "omiyanome-c06",
      "source_id": "omiyanome-s2",
      "quote": "The norito liturgy for this ritual contained in the Engishiki is a pacification blessing meant to ensure the peacefulness of the new palace built for imperial accession.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Liturgi Engishiki ritual itu memberkati istana penobatan baru dengan kedamaian.",
        "en": "Its Engishiki liturgy blesses the new accession palace with peace."
      }
    }
  ],

      "quote": "In the tale, the fox spirit appears as a woman carrying an infant and asks passersby for help.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul sebagai perempuan yang membawa bayi dan meminta bantuan.",
        "en": "It appears as a woman carrying a baby and asking for help."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "osan-kitsune-c04",
      "source_id": "osan-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "After handing over the child, the figure disappears; the child is later revealed to be a stone.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perempuan menghilang setelah menyerahkan bayi, yang ternyata batu.",
        "en": "The woman disappears after handing over the child, which proves to be a stone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "osan-kitsune-c05",
      "source_id": "osan-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "Several locations in the Eba area of Hiroshima are associated with the Osan kitsune legend.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa tempat di Eba terkait dengan legenda itu.",
        "en": "Several places in Eba are associated with the legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "osan-kitsune-c06",
      "source_id": "osan-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "A road known as Osan-dōri (おさん通り) is named after the figure.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jalan setempat memakai nama sosok itu.",
        "en": "A local road bears the figure’s name."
      }

    "en": "Oseichū denotes a speech-answering creature and a legendary affliction in China and Japan.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Oseichū menunjuk makhluk penjawab ucapan dan penyakit legendaris di Tiongkok serta Jepang. Kisah Tiongkok menggambarkan serangga dalam tubuh yang menjawab ucapan tanpa inangnya berbicara.",
      "en": "Oseichū denotes a speech-answering creature and a legendary affliction in China and Japan. Chinese accounts describe an internal insect answering speech without its host speaking.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "oseichu-c01",
        "oseichu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemakaian Tionghoa menjadikan suara penirunya kiasan bagi pengikut tanpa sikap kritis. Kisah Jepang memberinya pertumbuhan mirip mulut pada perut orang sakit.",
      "en": "Chinese usage turns its imitative voice into a metaphor for an uncritical follower. A Japanese account gives it a mouthlike growth on a sick person’s belly.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "oseichu-c03",
        "oseichu-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "oseichu-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%BF%9C%E5%A3%B0%E8%99%AB",
      "title": "応声虫",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "oseichu-c01",
      "source_id": "oseichu-s1",
      "quote": "応声虫（おうせいちゅう）または応声（おうせい）は、中国や日本の説話集・随筆集に見られる奇病、およびその病気を引き起こす怪虫。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Oseichū menunjuk makhluk penjawab ucapan dan penyakit legendaris di Tiongkok serta Jepang.",
        "en": "Oseichū denotes a speech-answering creature and a legendary affliction in China and Japan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oseichu-c02",
      "source_id": "oseichu-s1",
      "quote": "応声虫による症状（応声虫病）があらわれた人物の説話は、中国の『朝野僉載』や『文昌雑録』、『遯斎間覧』などに記述がみられ、本草書である『本草綱目』には応声虫に効果があったとされる雷丸（らいがん）や藍（あい）の解説文中にもその存在が言及されている。 応声虫が人体の中に入り込むと、本人は何もしゃべっていないのに腹の中から問いかけに応じた返事がかえって来るとされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Tiongkok menggambarkan serangga dalam tubuh yang menjawab ucapan tanpa inangnya berbicara.",
        "en": "Chinese accounts describe an internal insect answering speech without its host speaking."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oseichu-c03",
      "source_id": "oseichu-s1",
      "quote": "腹の中から虫が声を出すという症状を受け、中国では「自分の意見をもたず付和雷同した意見のみを言う者」を応声虫と揶揄して呼んだともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemakaian Tionghoa menjadikan suara penirunya kiasan bagi pengikut tanpa sikap kritis.",
        "en": "Chinese usage turns its imitative voice into a metaphor for an uncritical follower."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oseichu-c04",
      "source_id": "oseichu-s1",
      "quote": "日本においても、回虫などの寄生虫のように人間の体内に棲む怪虫によって引き起こされる病気であるとされ、人間がこの病気に冒されると、高熱が10日間ほど続いて苦しんだ後、腹に出来物ができ、次第にそれが口のような形になる。この口は病気になった者の喋ったことを口真似するため、応声虫の名がある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Jepang memberinya pertumbuhan mirip mulut pada perut orang sakit.",
        "en": "A Japanese account gives it a mouthlike growth on a sick person’s belly."
      }
    }
  ],

        "otora-kitsune-c02"

      "en": "Possessed people are said to recount the Battle of Nagashino as personal memories. A 1916 collection records cases from several nearby districts.",

        "otora-kitsune-c04"
      ]

  "cultural_context": null,

      "id": "otora-kitsune-c01",
      "source_id": "otora-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "おとら狐が人に憑くのは、本来長篠城の稲荷社の使いであったおとら狐が、長篠の合戦後に社を放置されたことを恨んでいるためといい、後にそれを鎮めるために長篠城の城藪稲荷におとら狐が祀られたと伝えられる。",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "statement": {

      "source_id": "otora-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "取り憑かれた者は左眼から眼脂を流し、左足が病むという。これは、かつて長篠の戦いにおいて鉄砲の流れ弾を受けた為と言い伝えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",

        "en": "The tradition links left-eye discharge and leg pain to the fox’s battle injuries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otora-kitsune-c03",
      "source_id": "otora-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "憑かれる者の多くは病人であり、憑かれると長篠の合戦のことや身の上話をよく語るようになるともいう。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang yang dirasuki konon menceritakan Pertempuran Nagashino sebagai ingatan pribadi.",
        "en": "Possessed people are said to recount the Battle of Nagashino as personal memories."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "otora-kitsune-c04",
      "source_id": "otora-kitsune-s1",
      "quote": "愛知県の郷土研究社が1916年に出版した『郷土研究』にはおとら狐に取り憑かれた人間のさまざまな様子が伝承として記録されている。",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ratoshikamuy adalah makhluk laut Ainu dalam penjelasan nama Ishikari melalui pertandingan kekuatan.",
    "en": "Ratoshikamuy is an Ainu sea creature in a strength-contest explanation of Ishikari’s name.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ratoshikamuy-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ratoshikamuy adalah makhluk laut Ainu dalam penjelasan nama Ishikari melalui pertandingan kekuatan. Gurita raksasa dan burung darat terkuat bertemu di muara Sungai Ishikari.",
      "en": "Ratoshikamuy is an Ainu sea creature in a strength-contest explanation of Ishikari’s name. The giant octopus and the strongest land bird meet at the Ishikari river mouth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ratoshikamuy-c01",
        "ratoshikamuy-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membutakan Huri dengan tinta dan melawan tarikan keluar dari laut. Pertandingan mereka berakhir tanpa pemenang.",
      "en": "It blinds Huri with ink and resists being pulled from the sea. Their contest ends without either contestant prevailing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ratoshikamuy-c03",
        "ratoshikamuy-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],

      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",

      "title": "ラートシカムイ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ratoshikamuy-c01",
      "source_id": "ratoshikamuy-s1",
      "quote": "石狩にまつわる地名伝説として、ラートシカムイと巨鳥フリカムイ（原文では「フリー」）の力比べの説話が伝承される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ratoshikamuy adalah makhluk laut Ainu dalam penjelasan nama Ishikari melalui pertandingan kekuatan.",
        "en": "Ratoshikamuy is an Ainu sea creature in a strength-contest explanation of Ishikari’s name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ratoshikamuy-c02",
      "source_id": "ratoshikamuy-s1",
      "quote": "説話によれば、海で一番力の強いのはラードシカムイ〔ママ〕、そして\"陸での力もちは片翼が七里もあるという巨鳥フリー\"とされていた。互いに怪力を自負して対立していたが、あるとき石狩川の河口で二頭（一羽と一杯？）が遭遇した。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gurita raksasa dan burung darat terkuat bertemu di muara Sungai Ishikari.",
        "en": "The giant octopus and the strongest land bird meet at the Ishikari river mouth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ratoshikamuy-c03",
      "source_id": "ratoshikamuy-s1",
      "quote": "蛸は墨を吐き、口を尖らせ、目を怒らせると、フリーは鋼鉄のような翼を広げて身構えると、水面上に出ていた触手をついばみ攻撃を開始し、ずるずると蛸を引き揚げにかかったが、頭までは水面現れなかった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membutakan Huri dengan tinta dan melawan tarikan keluar dari laut.",
        "en": "It blinds Huri with ink and resists being pulled from the sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ratoshikamuy-c04",
      "source_id": "ratoshikamuy-s1",
      "quote": "こんどは蛸が押収して、触手を折り曲げて引っ張ったが、鳥はふんばって耐えた（詳細は、「石狩」地名由来とまとめて後述）。けっきょく力は両者とも互角で、決着がつくことはなかったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertandingan mereka berakhir tanpa pemenang.",
        "en": "Their contest ends without either contestant prevailing."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rep-un-kamuy menguasai laut dalam mitologi Ainu. Ia dapat muncul sebagai orka. Penampilan lain berupa pemuda pembawa harpun.",
      "en": "Rep-un-kamuy governs the sea in Ainu mythology. He can appear as an orca. Another appearance is a young harpoon-bearing man.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rep-un-kamuy-c01",
        "rep-un-kamuy-c02",
        "rep-un-kamuy-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah berburu menyebutnya mengirim paus dan anaknya ke pantai manusia. Ia menerima manusia mengambil paus meskipun burung wren menuduh mereka. Persetujuannya terkait dengan laut berlimpah yang menopang manusia.",
      "en": "A hunting story has him send a whale and its young to human shores. He accepts people taking the whale despite a wren’s accusation. His approval is associated with an abundant sea that sustains people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rep-un-kamuy-c04",
        "rep-un-kamuy-c05",
        "rep-un-kamuy-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "rep-un-kamuy-c01",
      "source_id": "rep-un-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Rep-un-Kamuy is the Ainu kamuy (god) of the sea.",
      "locator": "Article text",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Rep-un-kamuy menguasai laut dalam mitologi Ainu.",

    },
    {
      "id": "rep-un-kamuy-c02",
      "source_id": "rep-un-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Rep-un-Kamuy is sometimes depicted as an orca.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dapat muncul sebagai orka.",
        "en": "He can appear as an orca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rep-un-kamuy-c03",
      "source_id": "rep-un-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "In other instances, he is a carefree, somewhat mischievous young man armed with a harpoon.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampilan lain berupa pemuda pembawa harpun.",
        "en": "Another appearance is a young harpoon-bearing man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rep-un-kamuy-c04",
      "source_id": "rep-un-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "In the story, he harpoons a whale and her young, and throws them ashore near a human village.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah berburu menyebutnya mengirim paus dan anaknya ke pantai manusia.",
        "en": "A hunting story has him send a whale and its young to human shores."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rep-un-kamuy-c05",
      "source_id": "rep-un-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Rather than growing angry, he laughs, saying that the meat belongs to the humans and they can do with it as they like.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menerima manusia mengambil paus meskipun burung wren menuduh mereka.",
        "en": "He accepts people taking the whale despite a wren’s accusation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rep-un-kamuy-c06",
      "source_id": "rep-un-kamuy-s1",
      "quote": "Moved by this display of piety, Rep-un-Kamuy assures the humans that the bounty of the sea will keep them from famine.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Persetujuannya terkait dengan laut berlimpah yang menopang manusia.",
        "en": "His approval is associated with an abundant sea that sustains people."
      }
    }
  ],

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Saho-hime adalah dewi musim semi yang dipasangkan dengan dewi musim gugur Tatsuta-hime. Puisi lebih awal mengaitkannya dengan Gunung Saho dan dedaunan musim gugur.",
      "en": "Saho-hime is a spring goddess paired with the autumn goddess Tatsuta-hime. An earlier poem associates her with Mount Saho and autumn foliage.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "saho-hime-c01",
        "saho-hime-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hubungan musim seminya mengacu pada letak timur Gunung Saho dan simbolisme Lima Unsur. Puisi menjadikannya penenun kabut musim semi, dibandingkan dengan pewarnaan musim gugur Tatsuta.",
      "en": "Her spring association draws on the eastern location of Mount Saho and Five Elements symbolism. Poetry makes her a weaver of spring mist, contrasted with Tatsuta’s autumn dyeing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "saho-hime-c03",
        "saho-hime-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "saho-hime-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E4%BD%90%E4%BF%9D%E5%A7%AB",
      "title": "佐保姫",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "saho-hime-c01",
      "source_id": "saho-hime-s1",
      "quote": "佐保姫（さほひめ）は、春をつかさどる女神である。秋の女神である龍田姫（立田姫、たつた姫）と対置される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saho-hime adalah dewi musim semi yang dipasangkan dengan dewi musim gugur Tatsuta-hime.",
        "en": "Saho-hime is a spring goddess paired with the autumn goddess Tatsuta-hime."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "saho-hime-c02",
      "source_id": "saho-hime-s1",
      "quote": "元は佐保山の神霊であり、948年の『陽成院一宮姫君歌合』では秋の歌に登場している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi lebih awal mengaitkannya dengan Gunung Saho dan dedaunan musim gugur.",
        "en": "An earlier poem associates her with Mount Saho and autumn foliage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "saho-hime-c03",
      "source_id": "saho-hime-s1",
      "quote": "五行説では春は東の方角にあたり、平城京の東に佐保山（現在の奈良県法華寺町法華町）があるためにそこに宿る神霊佐保姫を春の女神と呼ぶようになった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hubungan musim seminya mengacu pada letak timur Gunung Saho dan simbolisme Lima Unsur.",
        "en": "Her spring association draws on the eastern location of Mount Saho and Five Elements symbolism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "saho-hime-c04",
      "source_id": "saho-hime-s1",
      "quote": "竜田姫が裁縫や染めものを得意とする神であるため、対となる佐保姫も染めものや機織を司る女神と位置づけられ古くから信仰を集めている。古来その絶景で名高い竜田山の紅葉は竜田姫が染め、佐保山を取り巻く薄衣のような春霞は佐保姫が織り出すものと和歌に歌われる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi menjadikannya penenun kabut musim semi, dibandingkan dengan pewarnaan musim gugur Tatsuta.",
        "en": "Poetry makes her a weaver of spring mist, contrasted with Tatsuta’s autumn dyeing."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Samebito adalah manusia hiu dalam dongeng yang diceritakan ulang Lafcadio Hearn.",
    "en": "Samebito is the shark-person in a folktale retold by Lafcadio Hearn.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "samebito-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Samebito adalah manusia hiu dalam dongeng yang diceritakan ulang Lafcadio Hearn. Ia berkulit hitam, bermata zamrud, berwajah mirip iblis, dan berjanggut mirip naga. Dongeng menampilkannya sebagai sosok lembut yang dibuang dari Istana Naga.",
      "en": "Samebito is the shark-person in a folktale retold by Lafcadio Hearn. He has black skin, emerald eyes, a demonic face and a dragonlike beard. The tale presents him as a gentle exile from the Dragon Palace.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "samebito-c01",
        "samebito-c02",
        "samebito-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Air matanya yang seperti darah mengeras menjadi rubi. Catatan Hearn secara tegas menyebut manusia hiu ini laki-laki. Penuturan Hearn menghubungkan air mata permata dengan kaum penghuni laut.",
      "en": "His bloodlike tears harden into rubies. Hearn’s note explicitly describes this shark-person as male. Hearn’s telling connects the gem tears with the sea people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "samebito-c04",
        "samebito-c05",
        "samebito-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

    {
      "id": "samebito-s2",
      "published": null,

      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/34215/pg34215-images.html",
      "title": "Shadowings: The Gratitude of the Samébito",
      "publisher": "Project Gutenberg",
      "author": "Lafcadio Hearn",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "samebito-c01",
      "source_id": "samebito-s1",
      "quote": "is a creature that appears in \"The Gratitude of the Samebito\", a short story by Lafcadio Hearn.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Samebito adalah manusia hiu dalam dongeng yang diceritakan ulang Lafcadio Hearn.",
        "en": "Samebito is the shark-person in a folktale retold by Lafcadio Hearn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samebito-c02",
      "source_id": "samebito-s1",
      "quote": "It is described as a shark-like humanoid with inky black skin, emerald green eyes, a face like a demon's, and a beard like a dragon's.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berkulit hitam, bermata zamrud, berwajah mirip iblis, dan berjanggut mirip naga.",
        "en": "He has black skin, emerald eyes, a demonic face and a dragonlike beard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samebito-c03",
      "source_id": "samebito-s1",
      "quote": "Although frightening, the creature turns out to be a gentle being who, as punishment for a petty fault, has been expelled from the sea by his former employer Ryūjin.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongeng menampilkannya sebagai sosok lembut yang dibuang dari Istana Naga.",
        "en": "The tale presents him as a gentle exile from the Dragon Palace."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samebito-c04",
      "source_id": "samebito-s1",
      "quote": "When the Samebito learns that his master is on his deathbed, he begins to cry tears of blood which become precious rubies when they hit the ground.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Air matanya yang seperti darah mengeras menjadi rubi.",
        "en": "His bloodlike tears harden into rubies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samebito-c05",
      "source_id": "samebito-s2",
      "quote": "Literally, \"a Shark-Person,\" but in this story the Samébito is a male.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan Hearn secara tegas menyebut manusia hiu ini laki-laki.",
        "en": "Hearn’s note explicitly describes this shark-person as male."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samebito-c06",
      "source_id": "samebito-s2",
      "quote": "For when men of the sea weep, their tears become precious stones.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penuturan Hearn menghubungkan air mata permata dengan kaum penghuni laut.",
        "en": "Hearn’s telling connects the gem tears with the sea people."
      }
    }
  ],

      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-c01",
      "source_id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-s1",
      "quote": "山本五郎左衛門（さんもと ごろうざえもん）は、江戸時代中期の日本の妖怪物語『稲生物怪録』に登場する妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sanmoto Gorōzaemon adalah yōkai dalam narasi Edo Inō Mononokeroku.",
        "en": "Sanmoto Gorōzaemon is a yōkai in the Edo narrative Inō Mononokeroku."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-c02",
      "source_id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-s1",
      "quote": "平田神社所蔵の妖怪画では、三つの目を持つ烏天狗の姿として描かれているが、天明3年刊『稲亭物怪録』（慶應義塾大学三田メディアセンター所蔵）によれば、自分は天狗の類でも狐狸の類でもないと語っている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ilustrasi memberinya penampilan tengu gagak, meskipun ucapannya menolak identitas rubah atau tengu.",
        "en": "An illustration gives him a crow-tengu appearance, although his speech rejects a fox or tengu identity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-c03",
      "source_id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-s1",
      "quote": "そして7月30日に1ヶ月間の怪異の締めくくりとして、裃を着た40歳ほどの武士の姿で平太郎の前に姿を現して名を名乗り、自分は神野悪五郎（しんの あくごろう）と魔王の頭（かしら）の座をかけて勇気ある少年を100人驚かせるという賭けをしていたことを語った。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengungkap pertandingan yang memerlukan pemuda ketakutan dan menyebut Heitarō sebagai calon korban.",
        "en": "He reveals a contest requiring frightened young men and identifies Heitarō as a prospective victim."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-c04",
      "source_id": "sanmoto-gorozaemon-s1",
      "quote": "そして、自分はもう怪異を起こすことはないが、今後、平太郎が悪五郎に襲われた場合には、これを打ち鳴らせば自分が現れて力を貸すと言って、木槌を平太郎に与え、妖怪たちを引き連れて去って行った。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah gagal menakuti Heitarō, ia meninggalkan palu dan menjanjikan bantuan.",
        "en": "After failing to frighten Heitarō, he leaves a mallet and promises assistance."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Seiryū Gongen adalah dewi penjaga Daigo-ji di Kyoto. Tradisi mengidentifikasikannya dengan Zennyo Ryūō dan putri raja naga Sāgara.",
      "en": "Seiryū Gongen is the guardian goddess of Kyoto’s Daigo-ji. Tradition identifies her with Zennyo Ryūō and a daughter of the dragon king Sāgara.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seiryu-gongen-c01",
        "seiryu-gongen-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah membawanya ke Jepang dengan kapal Kūkai untuk melindungi Buddhisme esoteris. Nama tertulisnya yang terkait air dijelaskan melalui penyeberangan laut itu.",
      "en": "The story brings her to Japan on Kūkai’s ship to protect esoteric Buddhism. Her water-associated written name is explained through that crossing of the sea.",

        "seiryu-gongen-c04"

  "cultural_context": null,

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%B8%85%E7%80%A7%E6%A8%A9%E7%8F%BE",
      "title": "清瀧権現",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "seiryu-gongen-c01",
      "source_id": "seiryu-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "清瀧権現（せいりゅうごんげん または せいりょうごんげん）は、京都市伏見区所在の真言宗醍醐派総本山、醍醐寺の守護女神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seiryū Gongen adalah dewi penjaga Daigo-ji di Kyoto.",
        "en": "Seiryū Gongen is the guardian goddess of Kyoto’s Daigo-ji."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seiryu-gongen-c02",
      "source_id": "seiryu-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "清瀧権現は経軌（経文と儀軌）には説かれておらず、空海の御遺告によると、インド神話に登場する八大竜王の一、沙掲羅（しゃがら、サガーラ）の第三王女（一説には第四王女とも伝えられる）である善女（善如）龍王で、無熱地に住していた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi mengidentifikasikannya dengan Zennyo Ryūō dan putri raja naga Sāgara.",
        "en": "Tradition identifies her with Zennyo Ryūō and a daughter of the dragon king Sāgara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seiryu-gongen-c03",
      "source_id": "seiryu-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "後年、弘法大師空海が青龍寺を訪れ仏法を学んだ際、三昧耶戒を授けてほしいと空海に懇請したが許されなかった。空海が帰国する際に船中に現れて密教を守護することを誓ったため、京都洛西の高雄山麓に勧請された。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah membawanya ke Jepang dengan kapal Kūkai untuk melindungi Buddhisme esoteris.",
        "en": "The story brings her to Japan on Kūkai’s ship to protect esoteric Buddhism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seiryu-gongen-c04",
      "source_id": "seiryu-gongen-s1",
      "quote": "その際、海を渡ったので龍の字に「さんずい」を加えて日本では「清瀧権現」と敬称するようになった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama tertulisnya yang terkait air dijelaskan melalui penyeberangan laut itu.",
        "en": "Her water-associated written name is explained through that crossing of the sea."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Seoritsuhime adalah dewi pemurnian aliran deras dalam liturgi Pemurnian Agung. Liturgi menyebutnya membawa dosa mengikuti sungai menuju laut.",
      "en": "Seoritsuhime is a purification goddess of swiftly flowing streams in the Great Purification liturgy. The liturgy has her carry sins downstream toward the sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seoritsuhime-c01",
        "seoritsuhime-c02"
      ]
    },

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
