  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "seoritsuhime-c01",
      "source_id": "seoritsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "瀬織津姫、瀬織津比咩（せおりつひめ）は、古代の朝廷で行われた国家的儀礼の大祓で唱えられた大祓詞に登場する祓の女神であり、速い川の瀬に坐す神とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seoritsuhime adalah dewi pemurnian aliran deras dalam liturgi Pemurnian Agung.",
        "en": "Seoritsuhime is a purification goddess of swiftly flowing streams in the Great Purification liturgy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seoritsuhime-c02",
      "source_id": "seoritsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "「高い山や低い山の頂から勢いよく〔水が〕落下する速い川の瀬においでになる瀬織津比咩という神」が川から大海原へ罪を持ち出すと描写されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Liturgi menyebutnya membawa dosa mengikuti sungai menuju laut.",
        "en": "The liturgy has her carry sins downstream toward the sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seoritsuhime-c03",
      "source_id": "seoritsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "大祓詞に登場する祓の四神（瀬織津比咩を含め、うち三神は名前から女神）のうち、古典に登場するのは『古事記』の速秋津比売神（速開都比咩）だけであり、これらの神が古代の神祇信仰の中にあったのか、あったとすればどのような神格と位置づけられていたのかはわからない。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya ditafsirkan melalui arus deras atau aliran cepat.",
        "en": "Her name is interpreted through a torrent or rapid flow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "seoritsuhime-c04",
      "source_id": "seoritsuhime-s1",
      "quote": "速秋津比売神以外の大祓詞の祓の神については、大祓詞が載録された時より前からある神と考える学者がいる一方、大祓詞を分析し、大祓詞の創作時に作られた創作神名と推定する学者もいる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarjana berbeda pendapat apakah para dewa liturgi mendahului teks atau diciptakan untuknya.",
        "en": "Scholars differ over whether the liturgical deities predate the text or were created for it."
      }

    "id": "Shami Chōrō adalah yōkai shamisen dalam ilustrasi Edo Sekien.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shami Chōrō adalah yōkai shamisen dalam ilustrasi Edo Sekien. Komentarnya mengaitkannya dengan alat musik terbuang yang dahulu dimainkan seorang ahli. Peneliti menafsirkan namanya melalui peribahasa dari pemula menjadi tetua dan bagian Tsurezuregusa.",
      "en": "Shami Chōrō is a shamisen yōkai in Sekien’s Edo illustrations. The commentary links it to a discarded instrument once played by a master. Researchers interpret its name through a novice-to-elder proverb and a Tsurezuregusa passage.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shamisen-choro-c01",
        "shamisen-choro-c02",
        "shamisen-choro-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cetakan Yoshitoshi tahun 1865 dianggap meminjam desain Sekien. Uraian belakangan juga mengaitkan alat musik seorang ahli dengan perubahan menjadi yōkai. Uraian belakangan menghubungkan namanya dengan peribahasa tentang tingkat pemula dan senior.",
      "en": "Yoshitoshi’s 1865 print is considered to borrow Sekien’s design. The later account likewise links a master’s instruments with becoming yōkai. The later account connects the name with the proverb about novice and senior ranks.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shamisen-choro-c04",
        "shamisen-choro-c05",
        "shamisen-choro-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shamisen-choro-c01",
      "source_id": "shamisen-choro-s1",
      "quote": "三味長老（しゃみちょうろう）は、鳥山石燕による江戸時代の妖怪画集『百器徒然袋』にある日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shami Chōrō adalah yōkai shamisen dalam ilustrasi Edo Sekien.",
        "en": "Shami Chōrō is a shamisen yōkai in Sekien’s Edo illustrations."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shamisen-choro-c02",
      "source_id": "shamisen-choro-s1",
      "quote": "石燕による三味長老の解説文では、「是はこの芸にかんのうなる人の此みちに長たるものと用ひられしその人の器（うつは）の精なるべし」とあり、かつて名人に使われていた三味線が打ち捨てられ、妖怪化したものであると考えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komentarnya mengaitkannya dengan alat musik terbuang yang dahulu dimainkan seorang ahli.",
        "en": "The commentary links it to a discarded instrument once played by a master."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shamisen-choro-c03",
      "source_id": "shamisen-choro-s1",
      "quote": "また「沙弥（しゃみ）から長老にはなられず」という、順序を踏まずに上位にはなれないことを意味する諺も解説文の書き出しで引き合いに出されていることから「沙弥（しゃみ）」と「三味線（しゃみせん）」の掛詞と、『徒然草』第百三十四段にある三昧僧の話にある「不堪の芸を持ちて堪能の座に列り」などの文から発想された石燕の創作であると見られる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti menafsirkan namanya melalui peribahasa dari pemula menjadi tetua dan bagian Tsurezuregusa.",
        "en": "Researchers interpret its name through a novice-to-elder proverb and a Tsurezuregusa passage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shamisen-choro-c04",
      "source_id": "shamisen-choro-s1",
      "quote": "浮世絵師・月岡芳年は錦絵『百器夜行』（1865年）に石燕の三味長老を参考にしたと見られる絵を描いている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cetakan Yoshitoshi tahun 1865 dianggap meminjam desain Sekien.",
        "en": "Yoshitoshi’s 1865 print is considered to borrow Sekien’s design."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shamisen-choro-c05",
      "source_id": "shamisen-choro-s2",
      "quote": "Instruments which were once played by masters are the most likely to develop into yōkai.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan juga mengaitkan alat musik seorang ahli dengan perubahan menjadi yōkai.",
        "en": "The later account likewise links a master’s instruments with becoming yōkai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shamisen-choro-c06",
      "source_id": "shamisen-choro-s2",
      "quote": "The name also invokes the old Japanese proverb, “Shami kara chōrō ni wa nararezu,” meaning, “One cannot skip from novice to senior.”",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menghubungkan namanya dengan peribahasa tentang tingkat pemula dan senior.",
        "en": "The later account connects the name with the proverb about novice and senior ranks."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Shichimen Tennyo adalah dewi penjaga Sutra Teratai dalam Buddhisme Nichiren. Pemujaannya menyebar dari Kuon-ji di Gunung Minobu ke kuil Nichiren lainnya.",
      "en": "Shichimen Tennyo is a goddess guarding the Lotus Sutra in Nichiren Buddhism. Her worship spreads from Kuon-ji at Mount Minobu to Nichiren temples elsewhere.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shichimen-tennyo-c01",
        "shichimen-tennyo-c02"
      ]
    },
    {

      "en": "Her Buddhist identity is associated with either Kisshōten or Benzaiten. In a legend, a woman hearing Nichiren’s teaching becomes a red dragon and pledges protection.",

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",

  "claims": [

      "id": "shichimen-tennyo-c01",
      "source_id": "shichimen-tennyo-s1",
      "quote": "七面大明神（しちめんだいみょうじん）は、七面天女とも呼ばれ日蓮宗系において法華経を守護するとされる女神。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shichimen Tennyo adalah dewi penjaga Sutra Teratai dalam Buddhisme Nichiren.",
        "en": "Shichimen Tennyo is a goddess guarding the Lotus Sutra in Nichiren Buddhism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shichimen-tennyo-c02",
      "source_id": "shichimen-tennyo-s1",
      "quote": "七面天女は当初、日蓮宗総本山である身延山久遠寺の守護神として信仰され、日蓮宗が広まるにつれ、法華経を守護する神として各地の日蓮宗寺院で祀られるようになった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaannya menyebar dari Kuon-ji di Gunung Minobu ke kuil Nichiren lainnya.",
        "en": "Her worship spreads from Kuon-ji at Mount Minobu to Nichiren temples elsewhere."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shichimen-tennyo-c03",
      "source_id": "shichimen-tennyo-s1",
      "quote": "その本地は、山梨県南巨摩郡早川町にある標高1982mの七面山山頂にある寺（敬慎院）に祀られている神で、吉祥天とも弁財天ともいわれる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Identitas Buddhanya terkait dengan Kisshōten atau Benzaiten.",
        "en": "Her Buddhist identity is associated with either Kisshōten or Benzaiten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shichimen-tennyo-c04",
      "source_id": "shichimen-tennyo-s1",
      "quote": "すると、女性は笑みを湛え「お水を少し賜りとう存じます」と答えると、日蓮は傍らにあった水差しの水を一滴、その婦人に落とした。すると今まで美しい姿をしていた婦人は、たちまち緋色の鮮やかな紅龍の姿に変じて仰った。 「私は七面山に住む七面大明神です。身延山の裏鬼門をおさえて、身延一帯を守っております。末法の時代に、法華経を修め広める方々を末代まで守護し、その苦しみを除き心の安らぎと満足を与えます」と。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda, perempuan yang mendengar ajaran Nichiren menjadi naga merah dan menjanjikan perlindungan.",
        "en": "In a legend, a woman hearing Nichiren’s teaching becomes a red dragon and pledges protection."
      }
    }

    {
      "id": "Shii adalah yōkai Jepang mirip cerpelai yang konon menyerang sapi dan kuda. Kamus menempatkannya di Tsukushi dan Suō, memasuki rumah pada malam hari untuk melukai ternak.",
      "en": "Shii is a weasel-like Japanese yōkai said to attack cattle and horses. Dictionaries place it in Tsukushi and Suō, entering homes at night to harm livestock.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shii-yokai-c01",
        "shii-yokai-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Yamato Honzō menggambarkannya cerdik dan cepat sehingga sulit ditangkap. Tradisi Nagato menghubungkan serangan terhadap sapi dengan pelanggaran pantangan pertanian.",
      "en": "Yamato Honzō describes it as clever and fast, making capture difficult. A Nagato tradition associates attacks on oxen with breaking agricultural taboos.",

        "shii-yokai-c04"
      ]

      "source_id": "shii-yokai-s1",
      "quote": "シイ（青、𤯝）は、日本の妖怪。和歌山県、広島県、山口県、福岡県に伝わる。姿はイタチに似ており、牛や馬などを襲うという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shii adalah yōkai Jepang mirip cerpelai yang konon menyerang sapi dan kuda.",
        "en": "Shii is a weasel-like Japanese yōkai said to attack cattle and horses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shii-yokai-c02",
      "source_id": "shii-yokai-s1",
      "quote": "『日本国語大辞典』や『広辞苑』の記述によると、シイは筑紫国（現・福岡県）や周防国（現・山口県）などに伝わる怪獣で、その姿はイタチに似ており、夜になると人家に侵入し家畜の牛や馬を害する存在であるという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kamus menempatkannya di Tsukushi dan Suō, memasuki rumah pada malam hari untuk melukai ternak.",
        "en": "Dictionaries place it in Tsukushi and Suō, entering homes at night to harm livestock."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shii-yokai-c03",
      "source_id": "shii-yokai-s1",
      "quote": "『大和本草』の解説によると、周防国や筑紫国におり、やはり牛馬に害をなすもので、賢い上に素早いのでなかなか捕えることはできないとある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yamato Honzō menggambarkannya cerdik dan cepat sehingga sulit ditangkap.",

      "source_id": "shii-yokai-s1",
      "quote": "山口県 大津郡長門市では田で牛を使う際、5月5日に牛を使う、田植え時期に牛に牛具を付けたまま川を渡す、女に牛具を持たせる、5月5日から八朔までの間にほかの村の牛を引き入れるといった行為がタブーとされており、これらを破るとシイが憑いて牛を食い殺すといわれた。",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shiotsuchi adalah dewa Jepang dengan nama tertulis berbeda dalam kronik kuno.",
    "en": "Shiotsuchi is a Japanese deity with different written names in the ancient chronicles.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shiotsuchi-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shiotsuchi adalah dewa Jepang dengan nama tertulis berbeda dalam kronik kuno. Satu tafsir menjadikannya dewa arus dan pelayaran.",
      "en": "Shiotsuchi is a Japanese deity with different written names in the ancient chronicles. One interpretation makes him a deity of currents and navigation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shiotsuchi-c01",
        "shiotsuchi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tradisi Kuil Shiogama menyebutnya mengajarkan penangkapan ikan dan pembuatan garam. Ia mengarahkan Hoori menuju istana dewa laut dalam kisah mata kail yang hilang.",
      "en": "Shiogama Shrine tradition credits him with teaching fishing and salt production. He guides Hoori toward the sea deity’s palace in the lost-fishhook story.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shiotsuchi-c03",
        "shiotsuchi-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "shiotsuchi-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%82%B7%E3%82%AA%E3%83%84%E3%83%81%E3%82%AA%E3%82%B8",
      "title": "シオツチオジ",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shiotsuchi-c01",
      "source_id": "shiotsuchi-s1",
      "quote": "シオツチオジは、日本神話に登場する神である。 『古事記』では塩椎神（しおつちのかみ）、『日本書紀』では塩土老翁・塩筒老翁（しおつつのおじ）、また『先代旧事本紀』では塩土老翁と表記される。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shiotsuchi adalah dewa Jepang dengan nama tertulis berbeda dalam kronik kuno.",
        "en": "Shiotsuchi is a Japanese deity with different written names in the ancient chronicles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shiotsuchi-c02",
      "source_id": "shiotsuchi-s1",
      "quote": "名前の「シホツチ」は「潮つ霊」「潮つ路」であり、潮流を司る神、航海の神と解釈する説もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menjadikannya dewa arus dan pelayaran.",
        "en": "One interpretation makes him a deity of currents and navigation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shiotsuchi-c03",
      "source_id": "shiotsuchi-s1",
      "quote": "シオツチノオジを祀る神社の総本宮である鹽竈神社（宮城県塩竈市）の社伝では、武甕槌神と経津主神は、塩土老翁の先導で諸国を平定した後に塩竈にやってきたとする。武甕槌神と経津主神はすぐに去って行くが塩土老翁はこの地にとどまり、人々に漁業や製塩法を教えたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Kuil Shiogama menyebutnya mengajarkan penangkapan ikan dan pembuatan garam.",
        "en": "Shiogama Shrine tradition credits him with teaching fishing and salt production."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shiotsuchi-c04",
      "source_id": "shiotsuchi-s1",
      "quote": "海幸山幸の説話においては、ホデリ（海幸彦）の釣針を失くして悲嘆にくれるホオリ（山幸彦）の前に現れる。ホオリから事情を聞くと小舟（または目の詰まった竹籠）を出してホオリを乗せ、そのまま進めば良い潮路に乗って海神の宮に着くから、宮の前の木の上で待っていれば、あとは海神が良いようにしてくれると告げる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengarahkan Hoori menuju istana dewa laut dalam kisah mata kail yang hilang.",
        "en": "He guides Hoori toward the sea deity’s palace in the lost-fishhook story."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",

}

      "language": "ja",

      "author": null

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shirachigo-c01",
      "source_id": "shirachigo-s1",
      "quote": "白児（しらちご）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『画図百鬼夜行』などに描かれている日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shirachigo adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Shirachigo is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirachigo-c02",
      "source_id": "shirachigo-s1",
      "quote": "子供の姿で描かれている妖怪で、犬神とワンセットで描かれる。『画図百鬼夜行』では稚児髷（ちごわげ）の髪形をした子供、作者不明の絵巻『化物づくし』（湯本豪一所蔵）では着物をつけた仔犬として描かれている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya berbeda antara anak di samping Inugami dan anak anjing berpakaian.",
        "en": "Depictions vary between a child beside Inugami and a puppy wearing clothes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirachigo-c03",
      "source_id": "shirachigo-s1",
      "quote": "後者は大坊主という妖怪と向かい合って描かれているが大坊主は大きな犬が袈裟をまとったすがたで描かれており、石燕による犬神・白児とおなじくこちらも犬の要素が高い。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran anak anjing berhadapan dengan sosok anjing lain berjubah.",
        "en": "The puppy depiction faces another robed canine figure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shirachigo-c04",
      "source_id": "shirachigo-s1",
      "quote": "犬神の近くに従って描かれている様子から、昭和以後の妖怪図鑑の解説などでは犬神の家来・門人・弟子として仕えている存在として白児が紹介されることが多い。また、犬にかみ殺された子供がなる妖怪が白児で犬神の家来として命令を何でもきくなどとも解説されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Panduan yōkai belakangan menjadikan Shirachigo murid Inugami atau roh anak yang dibunuh anjing.",
        "en": "Later yōkai guides make Shirachigo Inugami’s disciple or the spirit of a child killed by a dog."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Soroban-bōzu adalah yōkai pemain sempoa dari Nishibetsuin di Kyoto. Kisah Kameoka menempatkan sosok mirip biksu di bawah pohon dekat Saikō-ji pada malam hari.",
      "en": "Soroban-bōzu is an abacus-playing yōkai of Nishibetsuin in Kyoto. A Kameoka account places the monk-like figure beneath a tree near Saikō-ji at night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "soroban-bozu-c01",
        "soroban-bozu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah mengidentifikasikannya sebagai tipuan tanuki atau roh calon biksu yang meninggal. Tradisi lain menjadikan pengunjung muda kuil tetangga sebagai pendiri Saikō-ji pada masa kecil.",
      "en": "Accounts identify it either as a tanuki trick or the spirit of a deceased novice. Another tradition makes the neighboring shrine’s young visitor the youthful founder of Saikō-ji.",

        "soroban-bozu-c03",
        "soroban-bozu-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

    {
      "id": "soroban-bozu-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%AE%97%E7%9B%A4%E5%9D%8A%E4%B8%BB",
      "title": "算盤坊主",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "soroban-bozu-c01",
      "source_id": "soroban-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "算盤坊主（そろばんぼうず）または算盤小僧（そろばんこぞう）は、京都府船井郡西別院に伝わる妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Soroban-bōzu adalah yōkai pemain sempoa dari Nishibetsuin di Kyoto.",
        "en": "Soroban-bōzu is an abacus-playing yōkai of Nishibetsuin in Kyoto."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "soroban-bozu-c02",
      "source_id": "soroban-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "西別院村笑路（現：亀岡市）では、夜中に笑路の西光寺の近くを通ると、寺のそばにあるカヤの木の下に坊主の姿で現れ、木の下で盛んに算盤を弾き始めるものを「算盤坊主」と呼ぶ。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Kameoka menempatkan sosok mirip biksu di bawah pohon dekat Saikō-ji pada malam hari.",
        "en": "A Kameoka account places the monk-like figure beneath a tree near Saikō-ji at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "soroban-bozu-c03",
      "source_id": "soroban-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "タヌキの仕業ともいわれているが、その寺では、かつて計算を間違えて和尚に叱られた小坊主が、その木で首を吊って自殺したと言われており、その坊主の霊とも言われている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah mengidentifikasikannya sebagai tipuan tanuki atau roh calon biksu yang meninggal.",
        "en": "Accounts identify it either as a tanuki trick or the spirit of a deceased novice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "soroban-bozu-c04",
      "source_id": "soroban-bozu-s1",
      "quote": "また、この西光寺の隣の素盞嗚神社でも、毎晩午前1時頃、境内の大木の下に少年の姿で現れて算盤の稽古を始めるものを「算盤小僧」という。一説によれば、この少年は西光寺の開山・萬安英種という和尚で、幼い頃に夜中に人知れず勉学に励んでいた姿だと伝えられている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi lain menjadikan pengunjung muda kuil tetangga sebagai pendiri Saikō-ji pada masa kecil.",
        "en": "Another tradition makes the neighboring shrine’s young visitor the youthful founder of Saikō-ji."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Takamen mewakili dewa pada perayaan tradisional di Takeshima, Kagoshima. Ritual memohon hasil panen, perlindungan dari penyakit, dan penyingkiran kejahatan.",
      "en": "Takamen represents a deity at traditional festivals on Takeshima in Kagoshima. The rituals seek harvests, protection against illness and removal of evil.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "takamen-c01",
        "takamen-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Topeng ritual tinggi dan mengerucut, dengan bagian mirip telinga dan hiasan kertas potong. Sosok berkostum menari mengikuti gong dan gendang serta memukul peserta perlahan dengan ranting.",
      "en": "The ritual mask is tall and conical, with ear-like parts and cut-paper decoration. The costumed figure dances to gongs and drums and gently strikes participants with brushwood.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "takamen-c03",
        "takamen-c04"
      ]

  "cultural_context": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "takamen-c01",
      "source_id": "takamen-s1",
      "quote": "Takamen (Japanese: タカメン) is a supernatural deity with a strange appearance that appears during the traditional Saku-odori or Toyomatsuri-odori festivals held from the 1st to the 2nd day of the 8th month of the lunar calendar on Takeshima Island (part of Mishima Village, Kagoshima District, Kagoshima Prefecture) in the Satsunan Islands of Kyushu, Japan.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Takamen mewakili dewa pada perayaan tradisional di Takeshima, Kagoshima.",
        "en": "Takamen represents a deity at traditional festivals on Takeshima in Kagoshima."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takamen-c02",
      "source_id": "takamen-s1",
      "quote": "It is featured as part of religious ceremonies aimed at ensuring good harvests, protection from illness, and the warding off of evil.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ritual memohon hasil panen, perlindungan dari penyakit, dan penyingkiran kejahatan.",
        "en": "The rituals seek harvests, protection against illness and removal of evil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takamen-c03",
      "source_id": "takamen-s1",
      "quote": "The mask of the Takamen is conical in shape and over one meter tall, adorned with large ear-like decorations and vibrantly decorated using kirigami (cut paper).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Topeng ritual tinggi dan mengerucut, dengan bagian mirip telinga dan hiasan kertas potong.",
        "en": "The ritual mask is tall and conical, with ear-like parts and cut-paper decoration."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "takamen-c04",
      "source_id": "takamen-s1",
      "quote": "During the ritual, the Takamen dances in rhythm with gongs and drums played by villagers, walking around while gently striking them with a bundle of brushwood (shiba).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok berkostum menari mengikuti gong dan gendang serta memukul peserta perlahan dengan ranting.",
        "en": "The costumed figure dances to gongs and drums and gently strikes participants with brushwood."
      }
    }
  ],

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Taniguku adalah dewa katak puru dalam mitologi Jepang. Ia mengarahkan Ōkuninushi kepada Kuebiko untuk mengenali dewa kecil yang datang dari laut.",
      "en": "Taniguku is a toad deity in Japanese mythology. He directs Ōkuninushi to Kuebiko to identify a small god arriving from the sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taniguku-c01",
        "taniguku-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kuebiko mengenali tamu sebagai Sukunahikona, anak Kamimusubi. Tafsir menghubungkan katak puru yang tersebar luas dengan pengetahuan seluruh negeri.",
      "en": "Kuebiko identifies the visitor as Sukunahikona, child of Kamimusubi. Interpretations link the ubiquitous toad with knowledge of the entire land.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taniguku-c03",
        "taniguku-c04"
      ]

      "title": "多邇具久",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "taniguku-c01",
      "source_id": "taniguku-s1",
      "quote": "多邇具久（たにぐく）は、日本神話に登場する神である。谷蟆、谷蟇の字を当ててヒキガエルを指す。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taniguku adalah dewa katak puru dalam mitologi Jepang.",
        "en": "Taniguku is a toad deity in Japanese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taniguku-c02",
      "source_id": "taniguku-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』における同段によると、大国主が出雲の御大岬（美保岬）にいたとき、海の向こうから小さな神がやって来たが、名を尋ねても答えず、誰もこの神の名を知らなかった。このとき、かかしの久延毘古なら知っているはずと注進したのが、ヒキガエルの多邇具久であった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengarahkan Ōkuninushi kepada Kuebiko untuk mengenali dewa kecil yang datang dari laut.",
        "en": "He directs Ōkuninushi to Kuebiko to identify a small god arriving from the sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taniguku-c03",
      "source_id": "taniguku-s1",
      "quote": "はたして、久延毘古によって、その小さな神の名は「神産巣日神の子の少彦名神」であると知らされる。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuebiko mengenali tamu sebagai Sukunahikona, anak Kamimusubi.",
        "en": "Kuebiko identifies the visitor as Sukunahikona, child of Kamimusubi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taniguku-c04",
      "source_id": "taniguku-s1",
      "quote": "谷蟆（ヒキガエル）は、地上のどこにでも生息しているため、「国土の隅々まで知り尽くした存在」であるとか「地上を這い回る支配者」と考えられていた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tafsir menghubungkan katak puru yang tersebar luas dengan pengetahuan seluruh negeri.",
        "en": "Interpretations link the ubiquitous toad with knowledge of the entire land."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tenaga-Ashinaga adalah raksasa dalam sejumlah legenda daerah Jepang. Mereka muncul sebagai satu raksasa, pasangan menikah, atau saudara dengan anggota tubuh luar biasa panjang.",
      "en": "Tenaga-Ashinaga are giants in several Japanese regional legends. They appear as one giant, a married pair or siblings with unusually long limbs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tenaga-ashinaga-c01",
        "tenaga-ashinaga-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah Gunung Chōkai menggambarkan serangan terhadap pelancong dan kapal. Kisah berbeda antara hilangnya mereka setelah doa dan penyerahan diri disertai penghentian kanibalisme.",
      "en": "Mount Chōkai accounts describe attacks on travelers and ships. Accounts differ between their disappearance after prayer and surrender with an end to cannibalism.",

      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%89%8B%E9%95%B7%E8%B6%B3%E9%95%B7",
      "title": "手長足長",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tenaga-ashinaga-c01",
      "source_id": "tenaga-ashinaga-s1",
      "quote": "手長足長（てながあしなが）は、秋田県、山形県、福島県、長野県、福井県などに伝わる伝説・昔話に登場する巨人。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tenaga-Ashinaga adalah raksasa dalam sejumlah legenda daerah Jepang.",
        "en": "Tenaga-Ashinaga are giants in several Japanese regional legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenaga-ashinaga-c02",
      "source_id": "tenaga-ashinaga-s1",
      "quote": "その特徴は｢手足が異常に長い巨人｣で各地の伝説は共通しているが、手足の長い一人の巨人、または夫の足（脚）が異常に長く妻の手（腕）が異様に長い夫婦、または兄弟の巨人とも言われ、各地で細部は異なることもある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka muncul sebagai satu raksasa, pasangan menikah, atau saudara dengan anggota tubuh luar biasa panjang.",
        "en": "They appear as one giant, a married pair or siblings with unusually long limbs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenaga-ashinaga-c03",
      "source_id": "tenaga-ashinaga-s1",
      "quote": "秋田では鳥海山に棲んでいたとされ、山から山に届くほど長い手足を持ち、旅人をさらって食べたり、日本海を行く船を襲うなどの悪事を働いていた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Gunung Chōkai menggambarkan serangan terhadap pelancong dan kapal.",
        "en": "Mount Chōkai accounts describe attacks on travelers and ships."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenaga-ashinaga-c04",
      "source_id": "tenaga-ashinaga-s1",
      "quote": "それでも手長足長の悪行は続いたため、後にこの地を訪れた慈覚大師が吹浦（山形県鳥海山大物忌神社）で百日間祈りを捧げた末、鳥海山は吹き飛んで手長足長が消え去ったという。また消えたのではなく、大師の前に降参して人を食べなくなったともいわれ、大師がこの地を去るときに手長足長のために食糧としてタブノキの実を撒いたことから、現在でも三崎山（にかほ市小砂川地域）にはタブノキが茂っているのだという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah berbeda antara hilangnya mereka setelah doa dan penyerahan diri disertai penghentian kanibalisme.",
        "en": "Accounts differ between their disappearance after prayer and surrender with an end to cannibalism."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tenjōkudari adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien. Ia menggantung terbalik dari langit-langit sebagai perempuan tua buruk rupa; beberapa kisah tidak memberinya tindakan berbahaya. Satu tafsir menghubungkan desain Sekien dengan ungkapan memperlihatkan langit-langit kepada seseorang.",
      "en": "Tenjōkudari is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection. It hangs upside down from a ceiling as an ugly old woman; some accounts give it no harmful acts. An interpretation derives Sekien’s design from an expression about showing someone the ceiling.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tenjokudari-c01",
        "tenjokudari-c02",
        "tenjokudari-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dongeng makhluk atap terpisah menggambarkan pelancong ditangkap dan dimakan. Uraian belakangan menyebutnya muncul terbalik pada malam hari untuk menakuti manusia. Uraian itu membiarkan arah pengaruh antara Sekien dan dongeng atap belum terselesaikan.",
      "en": "A separate roof-monster tale describes travelers being captured and eaten. The later account has it emerge upside down at night to frighten people. That account leaves the direction of influence between Sekien and the roof tale unresolved.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tenjokudari-c04",
        "tenjokudari-c05",
        "tenjokudari-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

    {

      "title": "天井下り",

    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-s2",
      "published": null,

      "url": "https://yokai.com/tenjoukudari/",

      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-c01",
      "source_id": "tenjokudari-s1",
      "quote": "天井下り、天井下（てんじょうくだり）または天井下がり（てんじょうさがり）は、鳥山石燕の妖怪画集『今昔画図続百鬼』に描かれている日本の妖怪。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tenjōkudari adalah yōkai dalam kumpulan ilustrasi Sekien.",
        "en": "Tenjōkudari is a yōkai in Sekien’s illustrated collection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-c02",
      "source_id": "tenjokudari-s1",
      "quote": "長い髪を振り乱した醜い老女が、家の天井から逆さまでぶら下がった姿として描かれている。書籍によっては、夜中などに家の天井から突然現れるが、特に人間に対して危害を加えることはない、などとされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menggantung terbalik dari langit-langit sebagai perempuan tua buruk rupa; beberapa kisah tidak memberinya tindakan berbahaya.",
        "en": "It hangs upside down from a ceiling as an ugly old woman; some accounts give it no harmful acts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-c03",
      "source_id": "tenjokudari-s1",
      "quote": "石燕の時代には、人を困らせるという意味で「天井を見せる」という言い回しがあったことから、石燕が言葉遊びで創作した妖怪との説もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu tafsir menghubungkan desain Sekien dengan ungkapan memperlihatkan langit-langit kepada seseorang.",
        "en": "An interpretation derives Sekien’s design from an expression about showing someone the ceiling."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-c04",
      "source_id": "tenjokudari-s1",
      "quote": "江戸時代の怪談集『宿直草』にある「甲州の辻堂に化け物のある事」によれば、甲州（現・山梨県）で山中のお堂の天井裏に化け物が住み着き、お堂に泊まった旅人を捕えて食らっていたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongeng makhluk atap terpisah menggambarkan pelancong ditangkap dan dimakan.",
        "en": "A separate roof-monster tale describes travelers being captured and eaten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-c05",
      "source_id": "tenjokudari-s2",
      "quote": "Every so often, in the middle of the night, it crawls out from the ceiling, upside-down, to scare people.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menyebutnya muncul terbalik pada malam hari untuk menakuti manusia.",
        "en": "The later account has it emerge upside down at night to frighten people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tenjokudari-c06",
      "source_id": "tenjokudari-s2",
      "quote": "However, it’s not sure whether this myth inspired Toriyama Sekien or rather was inspired by his work.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian itu membiarkan arah pengaruh antara Sekien dan dongeng atap belum terselesaikan.",
        "en": "That account leaves the direction of influence between Sekien and the roof tale unresolved."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Monster Danau Inba adalah makhluk tak dikenal yang dilaporkan dalam narasi zaman Edo tahun 1843.",
    "en": "The Lake Inba Monster is an unidentified creature reported in an 1843 Edo-period narrative.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "the-lake-inba-monster-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Monster Danau Inba adalah makhluk tak dikenal yang dilaporkan dalam narasi zaman Edo tahun 1843. Kisah berlangsung selama penggalian kanal dan bertahan dalam bentuk laporan pejabat.",
      "en": "The Lake Inba Monster is an unidentified creature reported in an 1843 Edo-period narrative. The account occurs during canal excavation and survives as an official’s report.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-lake-inba-monster-c01",
        "the-lake-inba-monster-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Catatan memberinya tubuh hitam, wajah mirip kera, dan cakar sangat panjang. Teriakannya yang mirip guntur konon membunuh tiga belas pejabat atau lebih. Yamaguchi Bintarō mengusulkan kisah itu menyindir proyek reklamasi pemerintah.",
      "en": "Records give it a black body, an apelike face and very long claws. Its thunderlike cry is said to kill thirteen officials or more. Yamaguchi Bintarō suggests the story satirized the government’s reclamation project.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-lake-inba-monster-c03",
        "the-lake-inba-monster-c04",
        "the-lake-inba-monster-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],

    {
      "id": "the-lake-inba-monster-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%8D%B0%E6%97%9B%E6%B2%BC%E3%81%AE%E6%80%AA%E7%8D%A3",
      "title": "印旛沼の怪獣",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "the-lake-inba-monster-c01",
      "source_id": "the-lake-inba-monster-s1",
      "quote": "印旛沼の怪獣（いんばぬまのかいじゅう）とは、江戸時代の1843年（天保14年）に今の千葉県北部である下総国の印旛沼に出現した正体不明の存在。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monster Danau Inba adalah makhluk tak dikenal yang dilaporkan dalam narasi zaman Edo tahun 1843.",
        "en": "The Lake Inba Monster is an unidentified creature reported in an 1843 Edo-period narrative."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-lake-inba-monster-c02",
      "source_id": "the-lake-inba-monster-s1",
      "quote": "印旛沼干拓事業の一環として、1843年（天保14年）に印旛沼の水を江戸湾に流す水路開削工事（堀割工事）が行われていた中で発生した騒動である。 怪獣事件を伝える記録は、当時の工事役人が天保14年9月2日（西暦:1843年9月25日）付で提出した報告書として残されている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah berlangsung selama penggalian kanal dan bertahan dalam bentuk laporan pejabat.",
        "en": "The account occurs during canal excavation and survives as an official’s report."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-lake-inba-monster-c03",
      "source_id": "the-lake-inba-monster-s1",
      "quote": "『年代記』や『密説風聞書』に残された記録によれば、怪獣は、体長1丈6尺（約4.8メートル）で全身が黒く、鼻の低い猿のような顔つきで、1尺（30センチメートル）もある爪を持っていた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan memberinya tubuh hitam, wajah mirip kera, dan cakar sangat panjang.",
        "en": "Records give it a black body, an apelike face and very long claws."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-lake-inba-monster-c04",
      "source_id": "the-lake-inba-monster-s1",
      "quote": "怪獣は、しばらく大きな岩の上に腰掛けていたが、突然、雷のような轟音を立てると、監視中の役人13人（またはそれ以上）が即死してしまい、生還した者達も重病になったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teriakannya yang mirip guntur konon membunuh tiga belas pejabat atau lebih.",
        "en": "Its thunderlike cry is said to kill thirteen officials or more."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-lake-inba-monster-c05",
      "source_id": "the-lake-inba-monster-s1",
      "quote": "山口敏太郎によれば、役人が大勢殺害される描写などから、幕府の印旛沼干拓に対する批判や皮肉を込めて、地元住民が創作した目撃談ではないかという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yamaguchi Bintarō mengusulkan kisah itu menyindir proyek reklamasi pemerintah.",
        "en": "Yamaguchi Bintarō suggests the story satirized the government’s reclamation project."
      }
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "toyohiwake-no-mikoto-c01",
      "source_id": "toyohiwake-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "『古事記』国産みの段において、伊邪那岐命・伊邪那美命が産んだ大八島国の一つである筑紫島は、伊予之二名島と同じく体は1つだが顔は4つあるとした。その2番目が豊国であるが、神の顔の名は豊日別であると記している。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Toyohiwake adalah wajah ilahi Toyo di antara empat wajah Pulau Tsukushi dalam Kojiki.",
        "en": "Toyohiwake is the divine face of Toyo among the four faces of Tsukushi Island in the Kojiki."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "toyohiwake-no-mikoto-c02",
      "source_id": "toyohiwake-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "他の3つの顔は筑紫国の白日別、肥国の建日向日豊久士比泥別、熊襲国の建日別である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wajah lainnya mewakili Tsukushi, Hi, dan Kumaso.",
        "en": "The other faces represent Tsukushi, Hi and Kumaso."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "toyohiwake-no-mikoto-c03",
      "source_id": "toyohiwake-no-mikoto-s1",
      "quote": "ただし、江戸中期の神道学者の度会延佳は、筑紫国や豊国といった国名は、いささか後世になってからの名称であって、神代には、国名が白日別、豊日別などの名称だったのであろう、筑紫島が9国に分かれ西海道と呼ばれるようになったのも（律令制が敷かれた）のちのことである、としている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Watarai Nobuyoshi menafsirkan nama ilahi itu sebagai nama daerah lebih tua.",
        "en": "Watarai Nobuyoshi interprets the divine names as older regional names."
      }
    }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tsuki no Usagi adalah kelinci yang terlihat pada corak Bulan dalam tradisi Jepang. Satu legenda terkait menjadikan kelinci sahabat monyet dan rubah.",
      "en": "Tsuki no Usagi is the hare perceived in the Moon’s markings in Japanese tradition. One associated legend makes the hare a friend of a monkey and fox.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tsuki-no-usagi-c01",
        "tsuki-no-usagi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia mempersembahkan dirinya dalam api untuk memberi makan tamu tua. Tamu itu, raja ilahi yang menyamar, menempatkan sisa kelinci di Bulan.",
      "en": "It offers itself in a fire to feed an old visitor. The visitor, a disguised divine king, places the hare’s remains on the Moon.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tsuki-no-usagi-c03",
        "tsuki-no-usagi-c04"

  "cultural_context": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tsuki-no-usagi-c01",
      "source_id": "tsuki-no-usagi-s1",
      "quote": "Les Chinois comme les Japonais voient dans les reliefs lunaires l'image d'un lièvre (appelé lapin lunaire ou lapin de jade).",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tsuki no Usagi adalah kelinci yang terlihat pada corak Bulan dalam tradisi Jepang.",
        "en": "Tsuki no Usagi is the hare perceived in the Moon’s markings in Japanese tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tsuki-no-usagi-c02",
      "source_id": "tsuki-no-usagi-s1",
      "quote": "Dans le passé, le lièvre s'est lié d'amitié avec le singe et le renard.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu legenda terkait menjadikan kelinci sahabat monyet dan rubah.",

    {
      "id": "tsuki-no-usagi-c03",
      "source_id": "tsuki-no-usagi-s1",
      "quote": "Lorsque les flammes s'élèvent et que les braises sont suffisamment chaudes, le lièvre se jette sur le bûcher en sacrifice et offre son corps comme nourriture au vieillard.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mempersembahkan dirinya dalam api untuk memberi makan tamu tua.",
        "en": "It offers itself in a fire to feed an old visitor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tsuki-no-usagi-c04",
      "source_id": "tsuki-no-usagi-s1",
      "quote": "Ce dernier, déclinant son titre de roi des dieux, réunit les restes du lièvre et les place sur la Lune afin que le sacrifice de l'animal soit visible de tous.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tamu itu, raja ilahi yang menyamar, menempatkan sisa kelinci di Bulan.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Umi-nyōbō adalah yōkai laut Izumo yang digambarkan membawa bayi.",
    "en": "Umi-nyōbō is an Izumo sea yōkai depicted with a baby.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "umi-nyobo-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Umi-nyōbō adalah yōkai laut Izumo yang digambarkan membawa bayi. Mizuki Shigeru menggambarkannya sebagai sosok gemuk bersisik dan bersirip yang makan ikan. Nama istri laut dalam Yamato Honzō mungkin berarti putri duyung dan dibedakan dari sosok Izumo.",
      "en": "Umi-nyōbō is an Izumo sea yōkai depicted with a baby. Mizuki Shigeru depicts her as a stocky figure with scales and fins eating fish. The Yamato Honzō sea-wife name may mean a mermaid and is distinguished from the Izumo figure.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "umi-nyobo-c01",
        "umi-nyobo-c02",
        "umi-nyobo-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah Uppurui menyebutnya mengangkat batu berat dan makan ikan asin sambil membawa bayi. Uraian belakangan memberi roh perempuan tenggelam dan istri biksu laut sebagai asal alternatif. Uraian belakangan menekankan gigi mirip hiu, kekuatan, dan bahaya.",
      "en": "An Uppurui tale has her lift a heavy stone and eat salted fish while carrying a baby. The later account gives drowned women’s spirits and sea-monk wives as alternative origins. The later description emphasizes sharklike teeth, strength and danger.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "umi-nyobo-c04",
        "umi-nyobo-c05",
        "umi-nyobo-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-s1",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%B5%B7%E5%A5%B3%E6%88%BF",
      "title": "海女房",
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "author": null
    },
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-s2",
      "published": null,
      "accessed": "2026-10-01",
      "url": "https://yokai.com/uminyoubou/",
      "title": "Umi nyōbō",
      "publisher": "Yokai.com",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "author": "Matthew Meyer"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-c01",
      "source_id": "umi-nyobo-s1",
      "quote": "海女房（うみにょうぼう）は島根県出雲地方に伝わる妖怪。磯女に類するもの。 == 概要 == 出雲の伝承では、赤ん坊を抱いた化物とされている。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Umi-nyōbō adalah yōkai laut Izumo yang digambarkan membawa bayi.",
        "en": "Umi-nyōbō is an Izumo sea yōkai depicted with a baby."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-c02",
      "source_id": "umi-nyobo-s1",
      "quote": "水木しげるによる妖怪画では、鱗とひれを持つずんぐり太った海女房が魚を食べている姿で描かれており、水木しげるロードの彫像も同様である。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mizuki Shigeru menggambarkannya sebagai sosok gemuk bersisik dan bersirip yang makan ikan.",
        "en": "Mizuki Shigeru depicts her as a stocky figure with scales and fins eating fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-c03",
      "source_id": "umi-nyobo-s1",
      "quote": "宝永時代の本草書『大和本草』には、「海夫人、海女房、俗にこれを人魚という」と記述があるため、人魚の異名とする解釈もあるが、これと出雲の伝承にある海女房とは異なるとの指摘もある。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama istri laut dalam Yamato Honzō mungkin berarti putri duyung dan dibedakan dari sosok Izumo.",
        "en": "The Yamato Honzō sea-wife name may mean a mermaid and is distinguished from the Izumo figure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-c04",
      "source_id": "umi-nyobo-s1",
      "quote": "とっさに隠れて様子を窺っていたところ、赤ん坊を抱いた海女房が家に入ってきて、片手で赤子を抱いたまま、もう片方の手でサバの塩漬けの桶の重石を軽々と持ち上げ、赤子と共に塩漬けを食べた。最後に「あの爺はどこだ。口直しに食いたかったのに」と言い残して去って行ったという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Uppurui menyebutnya mengangkat batu berat dan makan ikan asin sambil membawa bayi.",
        "en": "An Uppurui tale has her lift a heavy stone and eat salted fish while carrying a baby."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-c05",
      "source_id": "umi-nyobo-s2",
      "quote": "Umi nyōbō  are often thought to be the reincarnated spirits of women who drowned in the sea. They are also sometimes said to be the wives of umi bōzu.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan memberi roh perempuan tenggelam dan istri biksu laut sebagai asal alternatif.",
        "en": "The later account gives drowned women’s spirits and sea-monk wives as alternative origins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "umi-nyobo-c06",
      "source_id": "umi-nyobo-s2",
      "quote": "Their teeth are sharp and brilliant, like a shark’s. They are very strong, and very dangerous.",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian belakangan menekankan gigi mirip hiu, kekuatan, dan bahaya.",
        "en": "The later description emphasizes sharklike teeth, strength and danger."
      }
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ura-c01",
      "source_id": "ura-s1",
      "quote": "温羅（うら/おんら）は、岡山県南部の吉備地方に伝わる古代の鬼。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ura adalah oni kuno dari daerah Kibi di Okayama selatan.",
        "en": "Ura is an ancient oni of the Kibi region in southern Okayama."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ura-c02",
      "source_id": "ura-s1",
      "quote": "温羅とは伝承上の鬼・人物で、古代吉備地方の統治者であったとされる。「鬼神」「吉備冠者（きびのかじゃ）」という異称があり、伝承によると吉備には吉備津彦命（きびつひこのみこと）が派遣され退治されたという。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi menjadikannya penguasa Kibi yang dikalahkan Kibitsuhiko.",
        "en": "Tradition makes him a Kibi ruler defeated by Kibitsuhiko."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ura-c03",
      "source_id": "ura-s1",
      "quote": "温羅に対して矢を1本ずつ射たが温羅はその都度石を投げて撃ち落とした。そこで命が2本同時に射たところ、1本は撃ち落とされたが、もう1本は温羅の左眼を射抜いた。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kibitsuhiko melukai mata kirinya dengan menembakkan dua anak panah bersama.",
        "en": "Kibitsuhiko wounds his left eye by shooting two arrows together."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ura-c04",
      "source_id": "ura-s1",
      "quote": "すると温羅は雉に化けて逃げたので、五十狭芹彦命は鷹に化けて追った。さらに温羅は鯉に身を変えて逃げたので、五十狭芹彦命は鵜に変化してついに捕らえたところ温羅は降参し「吉備冠者」の名を五十狭芹彦命に献上した。これにより五十狭芹彦命は吉備津彦命と呼ばれるようになった。",
      "locator": "Article text",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengejaran mereka berubah dari burung pegar dan elang menjadi ikan mas dan burung kormoran sebelum Ura menyerah.",
        "en": "Their pursuit changes from pheasant and hawk to carp and cormorant before Ura surrenders."
      }
    }
  ],
